1.0 Introduction
War in the Pacific: Admiral’s Edition TM (“AE”), is a game where players may choose to direct the Japanese or Allied war efforts in one of several limited scenarios that cover a portion of the war and map of the Pacific, or you may choose one of the campaign scenarios that encompass the entire war and map of the Pacific.
AE was originally released with 3 full campaigns and 3 shorter limited scenarios.  The first official update added 4 scenarios.  Subsequent official updates have further expanded the list of official scenarios.
In addition to the officially released scenarios there are many other user created scenarios which are accessible via the AE forum. 
1.1 Game Manual
AE was released in July 2009 and since release it has had seven official updates which have resolved game bugs, introduced additional scenarios and provided many enhancements. This revised game manual incorporates those changes and enhancements introduced in the seven official updates.
1.2 Editor Manual
AE has an editor feature that allows you to create your own scenarios anywhere on the Pacific map.
Details on how to use the Editor are contained in the separate PDF Editor Manual file which is located in the Manuals sub directory of your AE installation.  Access to the Editor itself is through the Scenarios sub directory of your AE installation.
1.2.1 User Created Scenarios
The earliest start date for a user created scenario is 7 December 1941.  Scenarios may continue beyond 1946.  Any user created scenarios can only be saved to a slot after #25 as the first 25 scenario slots are reserved exclusively to official AE scenarios.
1.3 Installation
Please ensure your system meets the minimum requirements listed below.  To install the game, either double click on the installation file you downloaded or insert the game CD into your CD-ROM drive.  If you have disabled the autorun function for your CD-ROM or if you’re installing from a digital download, double-click on the installation archive file, then double click on the file that is shown inside the archive.  Follow all on-screen prompts to complete installation.
Minimum System Requirements;   
    • OS		Windows 98SE/ME/2000/XP/Vista
    • Processor	Pentium II 600 MHz CPU
    • Ram		512MB
    • Video Card	8 MB Video Graphics Card 16 Bit Color
    • Sound Card	DirectX Compatible Sound Card
    • CD-ROM		8x CD-ROM
    • Hard Drive	900MB Free HD Space
    • DirectX Version	DirectX 9c or Higher
Recommended System Requirements;
    • OS		Windows 2000/XP/Vista
    • Processor	1 GHz CPU
    • Ram		1 GB
    • Video Card	8MB Video Graphics Card 16 Bit Color
    • Sound Card	DirectX Compatible Sound Card
    • CD-ROM		8x CD-ROM
    • Hard Drive	900MB Free HD Space
    • DirectX Version	DirectX 9c or Higher
Newer Windows OS or multi-core systems with multi-threaded graphics may require use of some of the command line switches listed below in section 1.3.1.
1.3.1 Command Line Switches
The first official AE update introduced a number of command line switches to both improve performance on new multi-core computer systems (see section 1.3.1.1 below) and the visual appearance of AE (see section 1.3.1.2 below).  Additional command line switches to support wide-screen high resolution were added in the third official update. 
These Command Line switches are added to the Target Field in the Properties tab on the “Quick Start” shortcut to AE.  How to apply these performance and visual appearance switches is explained in section 1.3.1.3.  Players who have not installed the official updates (see section 1.5 below) do not have access to any of these command line switches.
1.3.1.1 Command Line Performance Switches
The Command Line Performance Switches are:
    • -SingleCpuStart	Starts AE in Single CPU Mode.
    • -SingleCpuOrders	Starts AE in Single CPU Mode. Processes the turn in Multi CPU Mode.
    • -cpu#		Specifies the core, in multi core systems, to be used.
    • -multiaudio	Fixes an audio timing issue on some multicore machines.
    • -dd_sw		Handles DirectDraw via Software. 
The -SingleCpuStart and -Single CpuOrders switches have been found to be useful on some multi-core systems, especially AMD processors.  Use either but not both switches.
The -cpu# (where # specifies the particular core) has been found to be very useful on some multi-core systems, especially Intel processors.
If used alone, the -cpu# switch will keep the game running on the specified CPU all the time.  If it is used together with the -SingleCpuStart switch, AE will use that core when starting the game.  If it is used with the -SingleCpuOrders switch, AE will stay in single CPU mode for starting and the orders phase but will run turns in multicore.  Using either of the Single CPU switches without the -cpu# switch defaults to CPU 1 when it is in single core.
In many instances the -dd_sw switch reduces interface lag and improves button clicking response times. 
As a general guidance, players with an Intel Dual Core system may benefit from this setup:
-cpu2 -multiaudio -dd_sw
Players with an AMD multi-core system may benefit from this setup:
-SingleCpuOrders -cpu2 -multiaudio
1.3.1.2 Other Command Line Switches 
The Command Line Switches are:
    • -px		Supports horizontal screen resolutions greater than 1024.
    • -pxf		Same as –px but used where graphic cards don’t natively support resolution.
    • -py		Supports vertical screen resolutions greater than 768.
    • -pyf		Same as –py but used where graphic cards don’t natively support resolution.
    • -deepColor	Enables 32 bit color. Intended for Full Screen Mode.
    • -noFire		Omits showing explosions and weapon shooting in Combat Animations.
    • -altFont		Enables alternate in-game font.(Lucida Sans Unicode)
    • -altFontSize	Adds one point size to text. Can cause merging of text. 
    • -colorBlind	Support for color blindness.
    • -w		Windowed mode.
    • -wd		Windowed mode, daily autosaves.
    • -ww		Windowed mode, weekly saves.
    • -fd		Full screen, daily autosaves.
    • -fw		Full screen, weekly saves.
    • -r		Show roads.
    • -autosave		Daily autosaves.
    • -archive		Put data reports in archive directory.
    • -skipVideo	Skips the intro video when starting the game.
    • -fixedArt		Will not use rotating images.
    • -cd		Enables the option for multi-day turns in a PBEM game in the preferences window.

Spanning mode is not supported and players should check that their video adapter and monitor is able to support their edited -px and -py values.  Two examples of widescreen settings are:
-f -px1920 -py1200 for a 1920x1200 monitor in full screen mode.
-w -px1680 -py1050 for windowed mode where the window is within the desktop.

The -deepColor switch has been found by many Win 8 and Win 10 users to resolve their slow scrolling screens.  It is usually best used together with the -dd_sw switch to resolve slow scrolling screens and slow button response problems.
The other visual appearance switches are self-explanatory.
1.3.1.3 Application of Command Line Performance and Visual Appearance Switches
To apply any of the switches shown in section 1.3.1.1.and 1.3.1.2 above, right click on the “Quick Start” shortcut to the game (for Win 7 users this is located in your Start Menu folder) and then click on “Properties”.  The Quick Start shortcut tab points to the game’s executable file which is named “War in the Pacific Admiral Edition.exe” in the “Target” field.  Do not edit the default desktop shortcut as that shortcut points to the file named “autorun.exe” which is not the game’s executable file.
The switches are added after the “War in the Pacific Admiral Edition.exe” in the “Target” field, with a space separating the executable file from the switches.  They are case sensitive and must have a space left between each switch.  It is also very important that a hyphen and not a dash be used.  The order of the switches is not important.
Note that the “Target” field is limited in the number of characters which can be displayed.  Click inside the “Target” field and use the keyboard arrow keys to scroll to the start/end of the field. 
1.4 Uninstalling the Game
Please use the Add/Remove Programs option from the Windows Control Panel or the Uninstall shortcut in the games Windows START menu folder to uninstall the game.  Uninstalling through any other method will not properly uninstall the game.
1.5 Product Updates
Matrix Games releases updates containing new features, enhancements, and corrections to any known issues.  All updates are available on the Matrix Games website and can also be downloaded quickly and easily by clicking on the “Update” link in your Game Menu or by using the “Update Game” shortcut in your Windows START menu folder for the game. To date eight official updates for AE have been released.
