Beta (preview) updates and other content is periodically made available to registered owners.  Keeping up with these special updates is made easy by signing up for a Matrix Games Member account. When you’re signed up, you can then register your Matrix Games products to receive access to these game-related materials.  Doing so is a simple two-step process:
Signing Up for a Matrix Games Member account IS A ONE TIME PROCEDURE; once you have signed up for a Matrix account, you are in the system and will not need to sign up again. Go to the Matrix Games website and click the Members hyperlink at the top. In the new window, select Register NOW and follow the on-screen instructions.  When you’re finished, click the Please Create My New Account button, and a confirmation email will be sent to your specified email account.
Register a New Game Purchase – Once you have signed up for a Matrix Games Member account, you can then register any Matrix Games title you own in your new account.  To do so, log in to your account on the Matrix Games website and Click on Register Game near the top to register your new Matrix Games purchase.
We strongly recommend registering your game as it will give you a backup location for your serial number should you lose it in the future.  Once you’ve registered your game, you can log into the Members section and click on My Games to view your list of registered games.  Each game title is a hyperlink that will take you to an information page on the game (including all the latest news of that title).  Also on this list is a Downloads hyperlink that takes you to a page that has all the latest public and registered downloads, including patches, for that particular title.
You can also access patches and updates via our “Games Section”. Once there select the game you wish to check updates for, then click the “downloads” link. Certain value content and additional downloads will be restricted to “Members Area” members so it is always worthwhile to sign up there.
Remember, once you have signed up for a Matrix Games Member account, you do not have to sign up again. At that point you are free to register any Matrix Games product you purchase.
1.6 Game Forums
Our forums are one of the best things about Matrix Games.  Every game has its own forum with our designers, developers and the gamers playing the game.  If you are experiencing a problem, have a question or just have an idea on how to make the game better, post a message there.  Go to the Matrix Games Website and click on the Forums hyperlink to find the forum of your choice.
1.7 Need Help
The best way to contact us if you are having a problem with one of our games is through our Help Desk.  Our Help Desk has FAQs as well as a dedicated support staff that answer questions within 24 hours, Monday through Friday.  Support questions sent in on Saturday and Sunday may wait 48 hours for a reply.  You can get to our Help Desk by going to 
MatrixGames.com. 
Thank you and enjoy your game! 
2.0 Getting Started
Playing AE is a task that may seem daunting at first with lots of information and the ability to direct the entire war in the Pacific all the way down to individual air squadrons, ships, and ground units.  With practice, the information screens and orders to be issued will become second nature.   However, playing this game will require a thorough knowledge of the rules, which will take some time.  This manual describes the various game menus and mechanics involved in the game.
The AE game system may seem overwhelming at first; there are myriad details to keep in mind and those details are spread out over many units and sub-units, as well as vast distances.  New players who are daunted at the level of detail present in a full-length campaign will find playing one of the shorter limited scenarios less daunting.  
2.1 Interface
The interface for AE is built around five types of input:
    • Filter switches that turn on and off associated options by left clicking on them.  On some filter switches a right mouse click accesses additional associated options.
    • Icon buttons which are found on the Menu Bar which runs along the top of the Main Map Display and also in the Hex Command Display which runs along the bottom of the Main Map Display.  There are also icon buttons found on the Base Information Screen.  A left mouse click on these icon buttons generally brings up lists or menu screens that may in turn take you to additional screens where you give orders.
    • Unit icons, which are most found on the Tactical Map, but also in the Hex Command Display and the Base Information Screen.  Unit icons represent individual units in the hex where the curser is located.  Click on a unit icon to open its Unit Information Screen to order the unit to undertake a particular action.
    • Directional arrows, which are the small left and right arrows next to many items.  These scroll through a list of available choices.  When there is a single arrow pointing to the right, it either launches another menu, as in “Form Task Force”, or it toggles orders such as “Automatic Convoy Off/On”.
    • Color Text generally indicates additional information is available.  Users can click on colored text and bring up lists or menu screens that may in turn take you to additional screens where you can give orders.
2.2 Hotkeys
The most used Menu Bar buttons have an associated hotkey which can be pressed instead of clicking on the button.  Some game actions and visual display selections also have an associated hotkey.  The great majority of these hotkeys are used on the Main Map Display during the Operational Orders Phase to access first tier information screens (see section 5) but there are also some hotkeys which are only available during the End of Turn Resolution phase.  The hotkeys are not case sensitive.
2.2.1 Main Map Display Hotkeys
The Main Map Display has the following hotkeys.  These hotkeys are available during the Operational Orders Phase.
[F2]	Toggles display between not showing all computer-controlled TF (auto-convoy and others), not showing human TFs, and showing all TFs on the Tactical Map
[F3]	Toggles on/off Plane Range Radius on the Tactical Map
[F4]	Toggles on/off Task Force Move Radius on the Tactical Map
[F5]	Toggles on/off Combat Animations during the End of Turn Resolution Phase
[F6]	Toggles on/off displaying hexside terrain details on the Tactical Map
[F7]	Toggles on/off Bad Weather (Clouds) Indicator on the Tactical Map
[F8]	Toggles on/off post combat battle summaries, but will retain combat reports
[F9]	Allows human player to enter the Orders Phase at next opportunity (AI games only)
[F12]	Immediately exits game to desktop, no confirmation required
[A]	Brings up the List All Land Based Air Units screen
[B]	Brings up the List All Bases screen
[C]	Brings up the Combat Report screen
[D]	View aircraft, troop, and vehicle Database
[E]	End the Orders Phase
[F]	Save the Game
[G]	Brings up the List All Ground/Land Based Units screen
[H]	Show the Auto Convoy System
[I]	Brings up the Intelligence Reports screen
[J]	Brings up Industry Management screen
[K]	Brings up the Weather Report screen
[L]	Brings up the Signal Intelligence Report screen
[M]	View Game Credits
[N]	Brings up the List All Naval Air Units screen
[O]	Brings up the Operational Report screen
[P]	Brings up the Preference and Options screen
[Q]	Quit game, confirmation required
[R]	Toggles on/off display of Road Networks schematic on the Tactical Map
[S]	Brings up the List All Ships screen
[T]	Brings up the List All Task Forces screen
[V]	View Ship Database
[W]	Displays Ownership of Hex and Hexsides on the Tactical Map
[Y]	Toggles on/off  display of Rail Networks schematic on the Tactical Map
[Z]	In  Orders Phase displays Air Search Arcs on the Tactical Map
[1]	Toggles on/off text for terrain type in each hex on the Tactical Map
[2]	Toggles on/off text for Zone Location in each hex on the Tactical Map
[3]	Toggles on/off weather forecast for each hex on the Tactical Map
[4]	Display original country ownership code for each land hex on the Tactical Map
[5]	Display supply path from base (yellow dots in PBEM, numeric value vs AI) 
[6]	Display submarine and ship patrol zones on the Tactical Map
[Ctrl - A]	Show the Large Strategic Map
[Ctrl - F]	Reduce main message delay in Turn Resolution Phase one increment
[Ctrl - J]	Toggles the Jump Map on or off in the full screen map mode
[Ctrl - S]	Increase main message delay in Turn Resolution Phase one increment
[?]	Centers Tactical Map on Home Base
[>]	Cycles through Next Task Force screen which lists ships in task force
[.]	Centers Tactical Map on Next Task Force
[<]	Cycles through Previous Task Force screen which lists ships in task force
[,]	Centers Tactical Map on Previous Task Force
[+]	Cycles through and Centers Tactical Map on Next Base
[-]	Cycles through and Centers Tactical Map on Previous Base

2.2.2 Intelligence Display Screen Hotkeys
The Menu Buttons on the Intelligence Display screen (see 5.1.4) have the following associated hotkeys to access these second tier Intelligence Reports.
[1]	Aircraft Losses
[2]	Group Reinforcement Schedule
[A]	Group Withdrawal Schedule
[3]	Aircraft Replacement Pool
[4]	List Top Pilots
[5]	Pilot Replacements
[6]	Ships Sunk
[7]	Ship Availability
[W]	Ship Withdrawal (Allied player only)
[8]	Ground Reinforcement Schedule
[G]	Ground Withdrawal
[K]	Ground Units Destroyed
[9]	Industry/Resource Availability
[0]	Industry/Troops/Resource Pool
[E]	Aircraft/Engine Production Pool (Japanese player only)

2.2.3 End of Turn Resolution Phase Hotkeys
During the End of Turn Resolution Phase, the computer processes the orders issued by the player during the Operational Orders Phase.  The player has no input during the End of Turn Resolution Phase but can use hotkeys to speed up messages.  Use of these hotkeys is different depending on whether it is a game against a computer or human (PBEM) opponent.
[Ctrl Z] (against AI)	Toggles on/off message delay effects for entire Turn Resolution Phase
[Ctrl Z] (in PBEM)	Toggles on/off non-combat animation message delay effects for entire Turn Resolution Phase
[Z]	Toggles on/off message delay effects until next combat animation
[Ctrl - P]	Pause Turn Resolution Phase
[Space Bar]	Restores combat animation message delay setting before use of [Ctrl - Z]

The [Esc] hotkey is also available to speed up (in PBEM) or skip (vs. computer) the current animation. 
2.3 Main Game Menu
This is where AE begins and where all of your important game-defining decisions will be made (although many can be changed in-game).  From here all game parameters are defined including the selection of sides, various realism options, and display preferences.  The Main Menu screen also records the current game version number just underneath the title.
Below is the Main Menu screen which appears after the opening AE video.



The options available on the Main Menu screen are listed in the following subsections 2.3.1-2.3.3.
2.3.1 Game Style Options
AE can be played human v computer, human v human (hot seat or PBEM) or computer v computer.  To select one of these options, click on one of the five white dial toggle switches and the dial will turn to indicate your selection.  The options are:
    • Japanese Computer – play as the Allies against a Japanese computer opponent
    • Allied Computer – play as the Japanese against an Allied computer opponent
    • Both Computer – observe the computer take both the Japanese and Allied sides
    • Head-to-Head – play another human in a hot seat game
    • Play by Email – play a game against a human via email (PBEM)
2.3.2 Game Parameter Options
Clicking on the following three red buttons opens additional screens.
    • Realism Options – takes you to the Realism Options Screen (see section 2.4) to select various rules affecting game play
    • Game Options – takes you to the Games Options Screen (see section 2.5) to select various options affecting game display
    • Preferences – takes you to the Preference Menu Screen (see section 2.6) to select various options regarding message delay, sound volume, and map appearance
2.3.3 Miscellaneous Options
The last two red buttons are:
    • Scenario Selection – click to take you to the list of scenarios available to play (see section 2.7)
    • Load a Saved Game – click to take you to your saved games
The red exit game toggle switch will, when clicked, exit AE and return you to your desktop.
2.4 Realism Options Screen
This screen provides nine toggle switches covering various rules which affect the play balance of the game.  Clicking on one of these toggles turns its status light (which is to the right of the switch) to either green or red.  A green light indicates the selection has been toggled on whereas a red light indicates the selection has been toggled off. See image of Realism Options Screen below.



All nine toggle switch options can be changed in-game (except during PBEM games) from the tool bar on top of the Tactical Map by clicking the Preferences and Options button.  The effects of these 9 rule options are detailed in sections 2.4.1- 2.4.9.
In addition to the nine rules options, the reinforcement schedules of either or both the Japanese and Allied side can be varied.  Details are provided in section 2.4.10
Clicking on the red Main Menu switch returns you to the Main Menu screen.
2.4.1 Fog of War
This switch controls the amount of information that either side can receive regarding the enemy. Historically, the commanders relied on spotty and often incomplete information on which to base their strike decisions.  In AE the player may select whether they wish to place themselves more fully in the same shoes as their historical counterparts.
When turned on, both players will receive incomplete or faulty messages and reports concerning enemy ship, troop, and air damage, as well as limited data on enemy bases.  If left off, all enemy units will be visible on the map at all times.
New players to AE may find some benefit in having this switch toggled off as they learn game mechanics.  Most experienced players will toggle this switch off
2.4.2 Advanced Weather Effects
When Advanced Weather Effects are turned off, the weather forecasts will always be clear on turn 1 and partly cloudy thereafter.  This will result in generally better weather.  When Advanced Weather Effects are turned on, the weather forecasts will have greater variability and generally result in worse weather.  Forecasts impact the actual weather in the hex, but any particular hex can have very bad weather even when the forecast is clear.  For more information on Weather refer to section 12.0.
2.4.3 Allied Damage Control
The Allied Damage Control switch toggles (on/off) if superior Allied damage control is factored into the combat and repair routines.  If toggled on, Allied ships will repair damage more quickly than Japanese ships. Allied Damage Control toggled on reflects the generally superior historical Allied performance in damage control.
2.4.4 Player Defined Upgrades (PDU)
This switch toggles (on/off) whether players can freely upgrade air units to other aircraft models, limited only by the aircraft type (fighter, light bomber etc.) of the unit, or whether only the specifically defined upgrade paths specified for that air unit and/or aircraft type must be followed.  With Player Defined Upgrades toggled off the defined upgrade path is generally that which occurred historically.
The decision whether or not turn this switch on is one of the most significant ones in terms of how a game is played.  
2.4.5 Historical First Turn
If Historical First Turn is toggled on in a game, the first turn in the scenario is automatically played without human input.  The opening automatic turn adopts the historical orders which occurred although the outcome will probably not correspond exactly with the historical result.  For example, if Scenario 1 is chosen together with the Historical First Turn, the Japanese Kido Butai (KB) will always attack Pearl Harbor on 7 December 1941.  The resulting losses in number and types of American ships sunk at Pearl Harbor will not match exactly the historical 7 December 1941 losses.  Similarly, the Japanese aircraft losses at Pearl Harbor will not match exactly their historical losses.  Human input for both sides then becomes possible on turn 2, 8 December 1941.
With Historical First Turn toggled off, both sides can issue orders on turn 1 of the chosen scenario and their opening moves need not follow the decisions made by their historical counterparts.
2.4.6 December 7th Surprise
If the player selects a scenario that begins on 7 December 1941, the December 7th Surprise toggle switch becomes available.  If toggled on, during the Morning Phase of 7 December 1941, the following occurs to represent Allied surprise:
    • Allied air units flying patrols (CAP, search etc.) have a 50% chance of not flying any aircraft.
    • If an air unit passes the 50% not flying test, the potential number of aircraft that will fly is reduced by 75%.
    • The Allies will launch no airstrikes.
    • Japanese Naval Task Forces may have enhanced first turn movement, depending upon TF settings in the scenario. (see Editor Manual for details)
    • Aircraft making a port attack during any 7 December 1941 phase will be much more likely to attack ships if there are at least 10 ships in the port.
    • Allied airfields suffer increased damage from any Japanese aircraft hits.
    • Allied ships suffer increased damage from any Japanese aircraft hits due to lack of Damage Control.
    • Vary Setup option has been disabled for AE.
2.4.7 Reliable USN Torpedoes
This switch affects those Allied torpedoes with initial dud rates of greater than 10%.  If toggled on, the historical problems with US Navy torpedoes are considered to have been immediately resolved, and the high dud rates for these weapons in the early part of the war do not occur, so the dud rate date reductions specified in section 6.4.2.1 are applied at the start of the scenario rather than on those specified dates in 1943.
Even with this switch toggled on, not all Allied torpedoes are affected.  British and Dutch submarines are equipped with torpedoes which do not have high dud rates.  Similarly, the old American S class submarines are equipped with a different torpedo which does not have the high dud rates which afflict the other American submarine classes in December 1941.
2.4.8 Realistic R&D
This switch applies only to a human Japanese player and involves whether aircraft factories currently producing aircraft can be converted into factories researching future aircraft models, and vice versa, without penalty.
If the switch is on, and the human Japanese player wishes to change the aircraft model currently in production at a factory his choice of new production is limited to another aircraft model currently available for production.  Similarly, if the human Japanese player wishes to change the aircraft model being researched by a factory his choice for R&D is limited to another model not yet available for production.
If the switch is off, no such restrictions apply.  This allows a human Japanese player a greater ability to switch production of their aircraft factories to any aircraft model regardless of whether those factories are representing aircraft production or R&D facilities.
The default position for this toggle is on as this is a more realistic representation of the historical Japanese aircraft industry.  It represents the difference between mass production factories and research and development facilities.  They are two different things and freely swapping between them is not realistic.
2.4.9 No Unit Withdrawals
If toggled on, no units will be withdrawn from the game due to a withdrawal date being set in the scenario data.  This reduces realism and the option is provided primarily to allow for play against a tougher Allied AI opponent.
2.4.10 Reinforcement (Allied/Japanese)
This option determines when reinforcements for each side arrive.  There are three available options to select for either or both sides.
    • Fixed – reinforcements arrive on the dates set in the scenario data.
    • Variable – reinforcements arrive at plus or minus 15 days from their fixed arrival date.
    • Extremely Variable – reinforcements arrive at plus or minus 60 days from their fixed arrival date.
Selecting one of the variable options introduces a degree of uncertainty to an opponent who is left uncertain as to the player’s current Order of Battle.  It also results in neither player in a PBEM game being able to see their reinforcement list from the Intel Screen on their first turn, but they will see their reinforcements commencing on turn 2.

2.5 Game Options Screen
This screen provides seven switches which affect the in-game reports and displays.  Just like the Game Realism Screen, each of the Game Options switches has an adjacent green or red light which indicates whether the switch is on or off respectively.


Again, these seven switch options can be changed (except for PBEM games) by clicking on the Preferences and Options button (Hotkey P) on the Menu Bar at the top of the Main Map Display. Further details on these options are provided in sections 2.5.1-2.5.7 below.
In addition to the toggle switches, this screen allows for the turn cycle and the AI Difficulty level to be selected.  Further details are provided in sections 2.5.8 and 2.5.9.  Clicking on the red Main Menu switch returns the player to the Main Menu.
2.5.1 Combat Reports
This switch toggles whether Combat Reports are created.  No Combat Reports are created for reading in the Combat Reports (see section 5.8) or SigInt (see section 5.5) screens if the toggle is off.  It is not recommended that this switch be off as even with Fog of War selected, the Combat Reports contain combat performance information.
2.5.2 Auto Sub Ops
If toggled on, the AI assumes control of all submarines.  The AI will create submarine task forces to undertake either offensive or defensive patrols.  These patrol zones may not match a human player’s priorities.
2.5.3 TF Move Radius
The TF Move Radius switch toggles whether range circles appear on the Tactical Map around the selected TF.  If toggled on, a green circle will appear around the task force indicating the maximum distance the task force will be able to travel at cruising speed during that turn, and a yellow circle appears to show the distance the task force can travel at maximum speed that turn.
2.5.4 Plane Move Radius
The Plane Move Radius switch toggles whether range circles appear on the Tactical Map around the selected air group.  If toggled on, a black circle will appear around the air group indicating the range for normal operations, and a red circle indicating the extended range for operations.  A blue circle indicates the maximum range of the air group
2.5.5 Set All Facilities to Expand at Start
When toggled on, the scenario starts with all the player’s bases expanding their facilities.  If toggled off the player will have to manually set bases to expand.
2.5.6 Automatic Upgrade for Ships and Air Units
If toggled on, eligible ships will upgrade to newer equipment and weapon configurations and eligible air units will automatically look to upgrade their outdated or inferior aircraft models with better aircraft models from the Aircraft Replacement Pool.  If toggled off the player will need to identify the eligible air units and arrange for their upgrades to occur.
Players should pay close attention to the potential ramifications of toggling this switch on.  With automatic upgrades on, players have no ability to prioritize which units receive the most modern aircraft models.  Allied players have fixed aircraft production and it is easy for air units scheduled to permanently withdraw, or permanently restricted to the Continental USA, to receive the upgrades thereby depriving frontline air units from flying competitive aircraft models against the enemy.
2.5.7 Accept Air and Ground Replacements
If toggled on, the player’s air and ground units will automatically accept any applicable items to fill out their rosters if they are missing any or have lost any in combat.  This will ensure that all your units will do their best to remain filled to capacity (subject to available supply) but will not give you control over what units you want filled out first.  If toggled off, the player will need to arrange for units to receive replacements.
As above with aircraft (section 2.5.6), players will do well to consider carefully using this feature.  Many devices are in short supply and having a permanently restricted unit upgrade its infantry squads to the latest version while front line units remain equipped with bolt action rifles may not be what the player wishes.
2.5.8 Turn Cycle
The Turn Cycle selection arrows choose how many days each turn is.  There are five options: 1 day, 2 day, 3 day, 4 day or continuous turns.  If continuous turns are selected, pressing the hotkey [F9] will suspend execution of combat resolution at the end of the current turn and return the player to the orders screen. (AI games only)
For games against the computer the Turn Cycle can be changed in-game.  For PBEM games the turn cycle can only be changed by the Japanese player who first needs to add the “-cd” switch to his command line before loading the turn.  Thereafter the Turn Cycle is changed in-game.
2.5.8.1 Continuous Play
If the Continuous Play option is selected for the Turn Cycle, at game start the players (human and AI) issue orders during their Operational Orders Phases.  The system then executes all orders simultaneously during the Day and Night Resolution phases.  The game will continue to process without stopping until a player interrupts the game by pressing the hotkey [F9].  When interrupted, the system finishes the current day’s Day Resolution Phase and then starts the Orders Phases.
This mode of play allows players to speed through several days when nothing important is happening.  It also allows for players to test any user created scenarios for bugs.
2.5.9 AI Difficulty
The AI Difficulty selection arrows provide for four levels of AI play.
    • Easy – the human player is given some advantages against the computer
    • Historical (default) – play is balanced with no advantages given to either side
    • Hard – computer is given some logistical advantages
    • Very Hard – computer is given some logistical and combat advantages
Players need to be aware that the AI is at a significant long-term disadvantage on the Historical (default) difficulty setting in any full length full map campaign scenario.  For the AI to remain competitive beyond the opening 6 months in a full campaign scenario against a human player, the minimum difficulty setting needs to be Hard.  For the limited length small map scenarios, the issue is mitigated by the scenario design and the Historical setting will be better balanced.
2.6 Preferences Screen
From this screen the game can be further customized by setting delays and Tactical Map details.
This screen has five switches, nine message delay selection arrows and four audio selection arrows.


The light next to the switches indicates whether the switch is on (green) or off (red).  Again, all the switches and selection arrows can be changed from the Menu Barr at the top of the Main Map Display clicking on the Preferences and Options button (hotkey P).  Further details on these options are provided in sections 2.6.1-2.6.8.  Clicking on the red Main Menu switch returns you to the Main Menu screen.
2.6.1 Map Style
This switch is not an on/off switch which is why there is no status light next to it.  The player flips the switch up or down.  If flipped up, the Tactical Map will display with hexes.  If flipped down, the Tactical Map will display without hexes.  With hexes displayed, it becomes easier to distinguish distance and location.  However, some players prefer the aesthetics look of a map without hexes and rely upon toggling on the TF Move Radius and Plane Move Radius switches (see sections 2.5.3 and 2.5.4 above) to gauge distances.
In addition to the original Tactical Map, there are user created map styles available for download on the AE forum.
2.6.2 Hex side Details
If toggled on, hex sides on the Tactical Map are shown with different colors.  The color of the hex side indicates which units can cross that hex side.  Details about allowable movement through hex sides are provided in section 4.2.2.  If toggled off all hex sides on the Tactical Map are grey.
On the Main Map Display the player can toggle hex sides on/off by pressing the hotkey [F6].
2.6.3 Map Scroll Delay
This setting determines the speed at which the map scrolls.  This is useful for slowing down the scroll rate for extremely fast computers.  Players who experience the opposite, namely an extremely slow scroll rate, should try adding some of the Command Line switches which are discussed in sections 1.3.1-1.3.2.
2.6.4 Delay Settings
The other eight delay settings refer to the length of pause for the player to read the messages or watch the animations for the appropriate events during the Turn Resolution Phase.  Some hot keys (see sections 2.2.1 and 2.2.3) can also be used to skip some of the messages or animations.  It is important to note that a lot of very important information is only contained in these messages/animations and is not in the associated Combat Reports.
2.6.5 Show Combat Animations
If toggled on, each battle will be displayed with an animated display.  The display will be accompanied by text describing the results of the action.  Depending on the delay settings chosen in the Preferences Screen, the messages may appear very quickly or the player may delay them so as to have time to read them all.  If toggled off, the Turn Resolution Phase will be much quicker, but the player will be denied access to much important information.
2.6.6 Show Combat Summaries
If toggled on, after each battle a short summary of the battle will be displayed showing the composition of the participants, any losses or damage suffered by either side (depending on your Fog of War settings, your reports of enemy losses may be exaggerated or false), and the overall results of the battle such as if ships were damaged or sunk during a Naval Attack Mission.
2.6.7 Show Clouds
If toggled on, the Tactical Map will show clouds over relevant weather affected hexes.  If toggled off, no clouds will be displayed but the hexes themselves will still be subject to any relevant weather effects.
2.6.8 Volume and FX
These values determine how loud the general sound, music, and sound effects (FX) are.

2.7 Begin a New Game
To begin a new scenario, click on the Scenario Selection button on the Main Game Menu screen.  The Scenario Menu screen then appears.


Two columns appear in the Scenario Menu screen.  The left-hand column lists all scenarios in the game’s scenario subdirectory, while the right-hand column shows an overview of the currently selected scenario.  Clicking on a scenario in the left-hand column brings up the corresponding overview of that scenario in the right-hand column
Nonofficial user created scenarios need to be allocated a number above 25 by the scenario designer and placed in the game’s scenario subdirectory before they will appear on the above Scenario Menu screen.  The first 25 scenario slots are reserved exclusively for officially released AE scenarios.
To play a scenario from the list presented in the left-hand column, first click on the desired scenario and then click the Select Scenario button.  The Scenario Details Screen then appears.

2.7.1 Scenario Details Screen



Click the Historical Briefing button to bring up a brief synopsis of how the selected scenario went for the player’s historical counterparts.  Clicking on the Scenario Menu button returns the player back to the Scenario Menu Screen where a different scenario may be selected.  Once satisfied with the chosen scenario, click on the Start Game button to play the chosen scenario.  Click on the Main Menu switch to return to the Main Menu screen without choosing a scenario to play.
2.7.2 A Note on Play-By-Email (PBEM) Games
In a PBEM game, it is the Japanese player who selects all the options on the Realism Options Screen (section 2.4), the Game Options Screen (section 2.5) and the Preferences Screen (section 2.6).  It is therefore important that both players agree beforehand on these settings.
When playing a PBEM game, both players will be prompted to create passwords.  The Japanese player then plots his orders and ends the turn.  The program will ask him to save the game, which he then emails to the Allied player.  The save should not be placed in Save Game slots 1 or 2.  Upon receipt of the email from his/her Japanese opponent, the Allied player loads the game, enters his orders and, when prompted, saves the game and emails it back.  When the Japanese player loads the file, the turn will automatically execute and create a combat replay file in Save Game slot 1.
For turn 2, the Japanese player again plots his orders, ends the turn, saves the game (other than in slots 1 or 2) and emails it to the Allied player.  From turn 2 onwards, the Japanese player must send both the save file and the combat replay file to the Allied player.  The Allied player can then first load the combat replay file and view the execution phase from the previous turn before loading in the save game file and entering the new Orders Phase.  These steps are repeated each turn with the Japanese player sending two files and the Allied player returning one file.
There is no need for the Japanese player to send to his Allied opponent any combat reports or any other text files.  The Allied player will generate his own combat reports when he runs the combat replay file.  If the Japanese player does not send the combat replay file, or the Allied player does not run the combat replay file, no combat reports will be generated for the Allied player.
At the end of a PBEM game, the Japanese player will need to send his password to the Allied player so the Allied player can load the final save file, view the execution phase and view the final victory score and end game status.
If starting a PBEM game with either variable or extremely variable reinforcements selected (see section 2.4.10), neither player will see their reinforcements in the Intel Screen on turn 1 but will be able to see them commencing on turn 2.
The sixth official AE update introduced an option to change a PBEM player’s password.  The option appears when ending the turn. 
3.0 Sequence of Play
AE is a turn-based game.  Each turn represents a number of days as specified in the Turn Cycle option (section 2.5.8) found on the Game Options Screen.  There are four separate Phases plus one subsidiary Phase in each 24 hour game period.  Within each Phase there are several sub-phases.  Depending on the number of days selected for the Turn Cycle, a turn may therefore comprise of only four plus one subsidiary phases if a 1 day turn cycle was selected, or twelve plus 3 subsidiary phases if a 3 day turn cycle was selected.  The opportunity for human input is limited to only the first two phases in the turn cycle with the computer processing the human inputs in the remaining phases of the turn. The 24 hour Sequence of Play is detailed below;
Japanese Orders Phase - The Japanese Player views the Tactical Map and gives orders to his units.  Air units may be instantaneously transferred between bases if the destination is within the air unit’s maximum range.  Various information screens can be accessed. The full range of Industrial Production orders can be issued.
Allied Orders Phase - The Allied player views the Tactical Map and gives orders to his units.  Air units may be instantaneously transferred between bases if the destination is within the air unit’s maximum range.  Various information screens can be accessed.  Very limited Industrial Production orders can be issued.
Night Resolution Phase - The Night Resolution Phase consists of 12 hours.  This Phase is resolved by the computer and requires minimal human interactivity.  A series of messages are displayed in the lower left quadrant of the screen indicating the progress of the resolution.
In a Human v Computer game, before the Night Resolution Phase commences, the human player will see in the lower left quadrant of the screen a display of messages which record the progress of the computer opponent in finalizing orders for its Orders Phase. The sub-phases of the Night Resolution Phase are listed below;

Load/Unload Sub-Phase - Task Forces load and unload cargos. Task Forces unloading over an enemy beach are fired upon by the defenders and in response return suppressing fire.  With Combat Animations toggled on, combat is graphically displayed. (Section 6.3)
Coastwatcher Search and Naval Reaction Sub-Phase - Coastwatcher’s attempt to sight enemy ships in port and nearby Task Forces. Task Forces that have “React to Enemy” movement orders automatically receive their new destinations. (Section 8.7) 
Auto Minesweeping Sub-Phase - Auto Minesweeping Task Forces conduct minesweeping operations. These are computer-controlled task forces. (Section 6.6.2)
Naval Movement Sub-Phase - Several interlinked actions occur during this sub-phase.  Task Forces will move towards their destinations and a meeting combat between Task Forces may result.  Submarine attacks against moving enemy Task Forces may result.  ASW Task Forces may attack enemy submarines.  With Combat Animations toggled on, all naval combat is graphically displayed.
Ships expend endurance points.  Ships are checked for system damage sustained due to being at sea.  Mine warfare operations (including being attacked by mines) are conducted. (Section 6.6)
Air Operations Sub-Phase - All night air missions are flown and air combat is resolved.  With Combat Animations toggled on, combat is graphically displayed.
Carrier Task Forces may launch aircraft assigned to night operations to attack enemy Bases and Task Forces.  With Combat Animations toggled on, all air combats are graphically displayed. (Section 7.2.1.3)
Surface Combat Sub-Phase - Remaining naval surface combat is resolved.  With Combat Animations toggled on, combat is graphically displayed. (Section 6.4.2)
Naval Bombardment Sub-Phase - Naval bombardment of bases and ground units are resolved.  With Combat Animations toggled on, combat is graphically displayed. (Section 6.4.3)
Ground Unit Movement - Ground units move towards their destinations. (Section 8.3)
Repair Ships Sub-Phase - Ships undergo repair both at sea and in port.  Includes possible additional Damage Control performed by the crews. (Section 14.2)
Base Repair/Construction Sub-Phase - Base repair and construction occur, including fortification building. (Section 9.5)
Supply Needs and Movement Sub-Phase - The supply needs of all units and bases are calculated and overland supply movement occurs. (Section 15.1)
Task Force Adjustment Sub-Phase - Crippled ships are automatically detached into a new Escort Task Force.  Certain Task Forces have their destinations set so that they will return to base. This sub-phase only occurs at night. (Section 6.2.3.1)
Day Resolution Phase - The Day Resolution Phase consists of 12 hours.  This Phase is resolved by the computer and requires minimal human interactivity.  A series of messages are displayed in the lower left quadrant of the screen indicating the progress of the resolution. The sub-phases of the Day Resolution Phase are listed below;
Load/Unload Sub-Phase - Transport Task Forces load and unload cargos. (Section 6.3)
Coastwatcher Search Sub-Phase - Coastwatcher’s attempt to sight enemy ships in port and nearby Task Forces. Task Forces that have “React to Enemy” movement orders automatically receive their new destinations. (Section 8.7) 
Auto Minesweeping Sub-Phase - Auto Minesweeping Task Forces conduct minesweeping operations. These are computer-controlled task forces. (Section 6.6.2)
Naval Movement Sub-Phase - Several interlinked actions occur during this sub-phase.  Task Forces will move towards their destinations and a meeting combat between Task Forces may result.  Submarine attacks against moving enemy Task Forces may result.  ASW Task Forces may attack enemy submarines.  With Combat Animations toggled on, all naval combat is graphically displayed.
Ships expend endurance points.  Ships are checked for system damage sustained due to being at sea.  Mine warfare operations (including being attacked by mines) are conducted. (Section 6.6)
Daytime Air Operations Sub-Phase - This sub-phase is divided into two pulses: a morning and an afternoon pulse.  Ordered daytime air strikes and combat is resolved in this sub-phase.  Air units given a Naval Attack mission may attack in both morning and afternoon pulses.  Air units given any other attack mission will only attack in one of the two pulses.  Recon, Naval Search and Air ASW missions are flown in both pulses.  Additional details regarding air combat and operations is found in chapter 7. Air units transport supplies and troops.  This sub-phase occurs only during the Day Resolution Phase.
Surface Combat Sub-Phase - Possible naval surface combat is checked, and combat resolved.  With Combat Animations toggled on, naval combat is graphically displayed.  Ships severely damaged in combat are automatically detached from their Task Force and placed in a new Escort Task Force.  Ship crews perform Damage Control. (Section 6.4.2)
Naval Bombardment Sub-Phase - Naval bombardments of bases and ground units are resolved.  With Combat Animations toggled on, combat is graphically displayed. (Section 6.4.3)
Repair Ships Sub-Phase - Ships undergo repair both at sea and in port.  Includes possible additional Damage Control performed by the ship crew. (Section 14.2)
Task Force Adjustment Sub-Phase - Crippled ships are automatically detached into their own Task Forces.  Certain Task Forces have their destinations set so that they will return to base.  Task Force fuel levels are assessed.
Ground Movement/Combat Sub-Phase - Ground units move towards their destination.  The Tactical Map centers on each moving ground unit. Combat between ground units is resolved.   Additional detail regarding ground combat and operations is found in chapter 8.  With Combat Animations toggled on, combat is graphically displayed.  This sub-phase occurs only during the Day Resolution Phase.
Ship Repair Sub-Phase - Ships undergo repair. (Section 14.2)
Base Construction and Repair Sub-Phase - Bases are built, repaired and fortified.  This sub-phase occurs only during the Day Resolution Phase. (Section 9.5)
Supply Requirements Sub-Phase - This sub-phase occurs only during the Day Resolution Phase.  Several logistical actions occur during the Supply Requirements sub-phase:
    • Base garrison requirements are checked
    • supplies, fuel, resources and oil are automatically moved overland to bases which have a demand for them
    •  Industrial Production occurs
    •  Base spoilage levels are determined
    • Air units repair aircraft and readiness levels are determined on basis of available aviation support and supply
    • Eligible ships, air units, and land units upgrade
Subsidiary Logistics Phase - At the conclusion of the 12 hours of the Day Resolution Phase, additional miscellaneous logistical tasks are finalized.  These include:
    • Training of ship crews
    • Human controlled task forces are disbanded
    • Aviation support is adjusted
    • Land units with replacements turned on receive replacement devices
    • Air units with replacements turned on receive replacement aircraft pilots
    • Reassignment and recovery of leaders 
    • Reinforcements arrive for new ships, air and land units

Several times during both the Night and Day Resolution Phases, the computer will check for submarine contacts and attacks. The computer also checks ships with fire and/or flotation damage for an increase/decrease in the fire and/or flotation level and for any additional damage caused by the fire and/or flooding. 
4.0 The Main Map Display
The Main Map Display is comprised of;
    • The Menu Bar (see section 4.1) which runs along the top of the screen
    • The Tactical Map (see section 4.2) which is below the Menu Bar and occupies most of the screen space of the Main Map Display
    • The Hex Command Display (see section 4.3) which is below the Tactical Map and to the left of the Jump Map
    • The Jump Map (see section 4.4) which is below the Tactical Map and to the right of the Hex Command Display
From the Main Map Display the player issues orders to his units, undertakes logistical tasks and accesses various information screens. 
Pressing the hotkey [Ctrl-A] toggles on the Strategic Map which replaces the Main Map Display screen.  The Strategic Map provides a zoomed-out overview of the progress of the war
4.1 The Menu Bar
Many of the commands you issue will be given by clicking on the Menu Bar buttons at the top of the Tactical Map.

There is a hotkey associated with each of the Menu Bar buttons (see section 2.2.1).  Between the Menu Bar buttons, information regarding the current player, weather forecast for the current hex, date and coordinates for the currently selected hex is provided.  For games played with ground stacking limits in operation, the ground stacking limit for the current hex is also displayed.
From left to right, the function of the buttons on the Menu Bar and their associated hotkeys are explained below.
      Save game, hotkey [F] Saves the game to a player selected slot from the available slots 003-199 inclusive.

      Choose Preferences and Options, hotkey [P] Brings up the Preferences and Options screen where the previously selected Realism (see section 2.4), Game (see section 2.5) and Preferences (see section 2.6) options can be altered.  	  Note, not all selections can be altered in an on-going game.

      View Aircraft, Troop and Vehicle Database, hotkey [D] Brings up the Aircraft and Ground Devices Database portal. (See section 5.4.1)

      View Ship Database, hotkey [V] Brings up the Naval Database portal. 	  (See section 5.4.2)

       View Intelligence Reports, hotkey [I] Brings up the Intelligence Display 	 screen. (See section 5.1)

 	 List all Bases, hotkey [B] Brings up the List all Bases screen. (See section 5.2.1)

       List all land based air units, hotkey [A] Brings up the List all Land Based Air Units screen. (See section 5.2.2)

      List all naval air units, hotkey [N] Brings up the List all Naval Based Air 	 Units screen. (See section 5.2.3)

      List all land units, hotkey [G] Brings up the List all Ground Units screen. (See section 5.2.4)

       List all active ships, hotkey [S] Brings up the List all Active Ships screen. (See section 5.2.5)

       List all task forces, hotkey [T] Brings up the List all Task Forces screen. (See section 5.2.6)

      Show the Industry Management screen, hotkey [J] brings up the Industry Management screen. (See section    5.3)

     Show the Auto Convoy System, hotkey [H] Brings up the Auto-convoy 	 System screen. (See section 5.7)

     Show the Large Strategic Map, hotkey [Ctrl-Z] Replaces the Main Map Display with the Large Strategic Map. (See section 4.6)

 	Display Combat Report, hotkey [C] Brings up the Combat Reports screen.  (See section 5.8)

      Display Operational Report, hotkey [O] Brings up the Operational Report screen. (See section 5.6)

       Display the Signal Intelligence Report, hotkey [L] Brings up the SigInt Report screen. (See section 5.5)

  	Display the Weather Report, hotkey [K] Brings up the Weather Report and the Cold and Malaria Zone map. (See section 12.0)

  	End Orders Phase, hotkey [E] Starts the End of Turn Resolution phases. (See sections 3.3-3.5)  

  	Centers the Tactical Map on the Home Base.  

  	Game Team Credits, hotkey [M].

  	Quit Game, hotkey [Q]
4.2 The Tactical Map
Most of the screen space of the Main Map Display is occupied by the Tactical Map
The Tactical Map is divided into hexagons (hexes) at a scale of 40 nautical miles (equals 46 statute miles) per hex.  The full Tactical Map used for full length scenarios is 230 hexes wide by 204 hexes high.  The outermost part of this area is reserved for the location of “off map” areas (see section 4.2.4) which are separate from the main map area.  Only the Allied player can access the “off map” areas. To scroll around the map, drag the mouse pointer in any direction and the Tactical Map will shift with it.  Alternatively click anywhere on the Jump Map in the lower right corner and the Tactical Map will center on that spot.
4.2.1 Terrain
Terrain on the Tactical Map is represented by both hexes and hex sides.  Both are used to represent various types of land and ocean terrain whose graphics are different.  Hotkey [1] toggles on/off a text description for each hex, while hotkey [F6] toggles on/off hex side colors which depict different hex side details.
Up in the top right of the Tactical Map, north of Lake Winnipeg, the following map art terrain Legend is provided.



4.2.1.1 Hex Terrain Types
The impact of land terrain on ground movement and combat is detailed in section 8.0.
The different types of terrain are:
Clear - open terrain, with excellent visibility and few places to hide.
Rough – hilly and/or broken terrain that is difficult for mechanized units to operate in. 
Forest – heavy foliage and nearly impassable terrain; this includes most of the terrain you’ll be fighting over.  Found in temperate climates.
Jungle – heavy foliage and nearly impassable terrain; this includes most of the terrain you’ll be fighting over.  Found in tropical climates.
Desert – arid, hot, and inhospitable terrain. In game terms these hexes are relatively easy to traverse and are therefore the same as Clear hexes for the purposes of combat and movement.
Swamp – thick marshland and wetlands, hindering movement greatly.
Forest and Rough – a combination of forest and rough terrain.  Almost as difficult to move through as mountain terrain and provides the same defensive bonus as a mountain.
Jungle and Rough – a combination of jungle and rough terrain.  Almost as difficult to move through as mountain terrain and provides the same defensive bonus as a mountain.
Desert and Rough – a combination of desert and rough terrain. Functionally the same as rough terrain.
Mountain – steep, high terrain that are impassable to mechanized units and nearly impassable to foot units.
Tropical Mountain – same as a mountain but located in tropical climates.
Sand Desert – desert areas filled with sand dunes.  Very difficult for mechanized units to move through.
Tundra – treeless areas in high latitudes.  Boggy in summer and snow covered in winter, they hinder movement greatly.
Heavy Urban – large, heavily populated cities that provide the best defensive bonus in the game. For movement purposes, Heavy Urban hexes are treated like Clear terrain and are assumed to have Main Roads going out in all directions.
Light Urban – small cities and large towns that provide less of a defensive bonus than Heavy Urban terrain. For movement purposes, Light Urban hexes are treated like Clear terrain and, like Heavy Urban terrain, are assumed to have Main Roads going out in all directions.
Developed – farms and other rural-type terrain in more modernized areas. Units treat these hexes like Clear terrain for movement purposes.  Developed terrain is assumed to have Main Roads going out in all directions.
Cultivated – farms and other rural-type terrain in less modernized areas. Units treat these hexes like Clear terrain for movement purposes. Cultivated terrain is assumed to have Secondary Roads going out in all directions.
Atoll – flat island terrain.  Special rules apply for atolls, see section 8.9.
Deep Ocean – dark blue water hex. Provides a benefit to submarines which are harder to detect in these hexes.  Minefields located in a Deep Ocean hex disperse more rapidly than does a minefield located in a Shallow ocean hex.
Shallow Ocean – light blue water hex. Submarines located in a Shallow Ocean hex are more vulnerable to detection.  Minefields located in a Shallow Ocean hex disperse more slowly than do minefields located in a Deep Ocean hex.
Icefield – frozen land hex.  Impassable to land units and ships.
Pack Ice – frozen water hex.  Impassable to ships and land units.
4.2.1.2 Land Transportation Links
Land Transportation Links are railways and roads that connect and provide faster means of movement between locations.  For the purpose of determining movement rates, they replace the movement rate of the underlying hex terrain type with their own movement rate.
Pressing the hotkey [Y] brings to the Tactical Map the schematics of the Railway Network and pressing the hotkey [R] does similarly for the Road network.  Both networks are separate from each other so two hexes may be connected by a railway and road, or a railway only, or a road only.  For travel between two hexes which are not connected by railways/roads, the movement rate is that of the intervening hex terrain types.
The graphical representation of Land Transportation Links are:
Major Railway.  High speed railway line, usually of standard or broad gauge. Appears as a dark grey line when the railway schematics are toggled on by the hotkey [Y]. To use a railway units must use strategic movement.
Minor Railway.  Slower, narrow gauge railway line.  Appears as a light grey line when the railway schematics are toggled on by the hotkey [Y]. To use a railway units must use strategic movement
Transcontinental Railway.  Special type of railway line found only in the North American “off map” areas (eastern Canada and eastern USA). The Transcontinental Railway provides for the movement of supplies from the interior of the North American continent to the West Coast without any losses due to distance.  Land units move between the two North American “off map” bases and the West Coast “on map” bases at a slower rate than if moving on a Major Railway.  The slower movement rate is to represent the compression of the actual railway lengths necessary to fit them into the available size of the map area.
Main Road.  All weather, usually asphalt, main roads.  Appears as a dark red line when the road schematics are toggled on by the hotkey [R].
Secondary Road.  A smaller or less well-built road than a Main Road. 	 Movement is faster than cross country travel.  Appears as a bright red line when the road schematics are toggled on by the hotkey [R].
Railway Trail.  A railway trail is a class of “road” that represents the ability of units using normal (non-strategic) movement to travel by foot or vehicle along a railway roadbed (Major or Minor) through otherwise rough terrain, and there are no better (Main or Secondary) roads present. While providing faster movement than when no roads or railway roadbeds are present at all, travel along railway trails is slower than travelling along Main or Secondary roads (and much slower than using railway lines for Strategic Movement.
When using Strategic Movement (which represents train transport) to travel along railway lines, units must be in Strategic Move mode and choose a base connected to the same railway network as a destination.
Destroyed Railway. A railway line that is now non-operational (e.g., the tracks are removed or washed away) and can no longer be used for strategic movement. The roadbed still acts as a railway trail.
4.2.1.3 Hex Side Barrier Terrain
There are five distinct types of hex side barriers which affect movement.  Players should note that these barriers do not reside within the hex but only in the hex side itself. The five hex side types are;
Reef- This is a hex side which represents built up coral that hinders naval movement. Coral Reef hex sides are impassable to all naval units except for ships that have a tonnage of 100 tons or less.  They are easily identified on the Tactical Map by a red hex side when the hotkey [F6] is toggled on.  An example of a Reef hex side is found in the Palk Strait Passage between India and Ceylon.
Impassable Mountain- The highest, most rugged mountain chains, such as the Himalayas are shown with a red hex side when the hotkey [F6] is toggled on.  No ground units may cross the red hex sides on these mountains. 
Navigable River- This is a Maritime Transportation Link (see section 4.2.1.4.2). A Navigable River is a river large enough to be navigable to both small and medium sized ships.  Technically Task Forces sail in a zig zag manner across the purple river hex sides.  A Navigable River is also a hex side barrier which operates exactly as a Major River.
Major River- Rivers provide a military benefit to defenders.  Land units which cross a river hex side (shown as purple with hotkey [F6] toggled on) into an enemy occupied hex, will suffer increased disruption.  The only difference with a Minor River is slightly different map art.
Minor River- Rivers provide a military benefit to defenders.  Land units which cross a river hex side (shown as purple with hotkey [F6] toggled on) into an enemy occupied hex, will suffer increased disruption.  The only difference with a Major River is slightly different map art.
4.2.1.4 Allowable Movement through Hex Sides
In AE, movement is across hex sides, not along hex boundaries.  The color of a hex side (if toggled on with hotkey [F6]) indicates whether land units or ships can cross that hex side.
The five hex side colors and the units which can cross these hex sides are:
    • Blue – 	Ocean/Lake hex side. Naval units only.
    • Green – 	Land hex side. Ground units only.
    • Purple – 	River hex side. Naval units only if Navigable River. (section 4.2.1.5.2) 
    • Red – 	Impassable (or reef) hex side. No Ground or Naval units except Naval units under 100 tons. (section 4.2.1.3)
    • White – 	Land and Ocean hex sides. Both Ground and Naval units may pass. 
4.2.1.5 Maritime Transportation Links
There are three different Maritime Transportation Link types; two are found on the Tactical Map and the third is found only in the “off map” area.  The “on map” types derive their special qualities from their hex sides whereas for the third, standard naval movement is changed slightly due to the special features of “off map” areas.
4.2.1.5.1 Tactical Map Straits
A separate attribute of some hex sides is the presence of a “strait” or narrow channel, through which ships must pass to move from one hex to an adjacent hex when performing naval movement.  There are two classes of “strait” on the Tactical Map - “wide” straits and “narrow” straits.  There is no map art which denotes the existence of a strait however a list of narrow straits is provided in section 20.3 Appendix C. 
Task Force movement through a strait can be affected in several ways:
    • Vulnerability to mines.  Task Forces moving through straits are more likely to hit mines that are located in the hexes either side of the strait hex side.
    • Vulnerability to attack by Coastal Guns.  Task Forces moving through straits are more likely to be attacked by coastal guns that are located in the hexes either side of the strait hex side.
    • Naval Reaction.  Task Forces with a “React to Enemy” movement order will not react through a strait towards an enemy Task Force if there are any known CD defenses in the strait.

The effects of straits on naval movement are more pronounced for narrow straits and less pronounced for wide straits.  Players should not order Task Forces to cross narrow straits unless it is known enemy minefields and CD defenses are minimal.
4.2.1.5.2 Tactical Map Navigable Rivers
A Navigable River is a river that ships may “sail” on.  Technically the ships cross the purple river hex sides and therefore sail in a zig zag manner.  Ships may not “sail” on major or minor rivers even though they too have purple hex sides.  An example of a Navigable River is the Yangtze River from Shanghai to Wuchang.  Beyond Wuchang, the Yangtze becomes a Major River only.
Navigable Rivers impose the following limitations.
    • Only ships with tonnages of 15,000 tons or less can sail on a Navigable River
    • The river hex sides have effects on naval units that are similar to the effects of narrow straits meaning that Task Forces are more vulnerable to mines and CD defenses
    • Unlike narrow straits, a Task Force with a “React to Enemy” movement order will react towards an enemy Task Force but only if the range between them is no more than 1 hex

For ground units, Navigable Rivers act in the same way as any other river. 
4.2.1.5.3 Off Map Maritime Transport Links
These are discussed in greater detail in section 6.2.10 but are mentioned here so that their map art can be identified.  As these Maritime Transport Links are located in the “off map” areas, they cannot be targeted for attack, nor entered into, by Japanese units.  Only the Allied player can access them. Examples are pictured in the Terrain Legend.
Transit zone – sea connection found only in the “off map” area.  Connects off map bases to other off map bases.  Allows Task Forces to travel directly between two connected off map bases.
Holding box – location of Task Forces travelling “off map”.  Task Forces moving to or from an “off map” base remain in the Holding box until they complete that part of their travel.
4.2.2 Tactical Map Icons
Multiple different icons may be present in a hex on the Tactical Map.  Icons exist for land units, bases, operational airfields, anchored ships, task forces, minefields, and to flag certain events.  The depth of color of land units, operational airfields and anchored ships provides a visual clue as to the strength of enemy forces present in the hex; a darker shade of color usually indicates stronger or more forces whilst a lighter shade of color usually indicate weaker or fewer forces.  
4.2.2.1 Land Unit Icons
Each major nationality has a standard color which is generally applied to their unit icons.
American (includes Filipino)	Olive
Australian	Forest Green
British Empire and Canadian	Tan
Chinese	Yellow
Dutch (Hex Command display)	Grey
Dutch (Tactical Map)	Olive
French	Sky Blue
Japanese	Red
New Zealand	Dark Blue
Soviet	Brownish Red

Minor nationalities share colors with their associated major nationality.
The land unit icons used on the Tactical Map are:
  British, Indian, Canadian, Commonwealth land unit
  Chinese land unit
  Soviet land unit
  US Army, US Navy, US Marines, Filipino, Dutch land unit
  Australian land unit
  New Zealand land unit
  French land unit
  Japanese land unit
Note that while the type of land unit is distinguished with different graphics on the Hex Command Display (see section 4.3), all land units are shown on the Tactical Map with the same graphic.  On the Tactical Map the only distinction shown is the nationality of the land unit.
Land units can be located inside or outside a base.  The position in a hex of a land unit icon is dependent on whether it is an Allied or Japanese land unit.  Allied land unit icons are found in the lower left of a hex while Japanese land unit icons are found in the lower right of a hex.
Each hex can display a maximum of two land unit icons only; one to indicate the presence in the hex of Allied land units and the other to indicate the presence in the hex of Japanese land units.
4.2.2.2 Base Icons
Bases, which have developed their airfield or port to level 1, are shown on the Tactical Map with one of the following national icons.  The nationality indicates the ownership of the base, not the nationality of the units present at the base.
  American, Filipino base

  Australian base

  British, Commonwealth, Indian base

  Canadian base

  Chinese base

  Dutch base

  French base

  Japanese base

  New Zealand base

  Soviet base

Bases, which have not yet developed an airfield or port to level 1 capacity are not displayed on the Tactical Map with one of the above national icons.  They are instead displayed as a dot in the color of the national land unit icon and are referred to as a “dot base”.  Once the airfield or port at a “dot base” is developed to level 1 capacity, the dot icon is replaced with one of the above Base Icons.
Hexes which do not have present a Base Icon (including the dot icon) can never have a base built.
4.2.2.3 Operational Airfields Icon
Airfields can only exist inside a base.  All bases can develop an airfield but not all bases start a scenario with a developed airfield.  Airfields empty of aircraft are not displayed on the Tactical Map.  Only operational airfields which have some aircraft operating from that airfield are displayed with this icon.


The operational airfield icon is found in the upper right of the hex.  The color of the icon varies depending on who owns the operational airfield, not on the nationality of the air units based there.  Thus, a Chinese base with an operational airfield will have a yellow operational airfield icon and a Japanese base with an operational airfield will have a red operational airfield icon. 
4.2.2.4 Anchored Ships Icon
Ports can only exist in a base which has access to the ocean.  Ships which are disbanded in a port are considered to be anchored.  In game terms, ports which have not yet been developed to level 1 capacity are considered to be an anchorage.
Ports (or anchorages) with no anchored ships in them are not displayed on the Tactical Map.  Where there are anchored ships the following icon is displayed on the Tactical Map.


The anchored ships icon is found in the upper left of the hex.  The color of the icon varies depending on who owns the port, not on the nationality of the ships anchored there.  Thus an American base with ships anchored in the port will have a green anchored ships icon and a Japanese base with ships in anchored in the port will have a red anchored ships icon.
4.2.2.5 Task Force Icons
Task Force Icons indicate the nationality of the flagship following the same color assignments for national Land Unit Icons.  If it is the sole Task Force for that side in the hex, the type of mission assigned to that Task Force is also indicated.

  Air Combat, CV Escort. (It is colored green because the flag ship is a US Navy ship.)

  Surface Combat, Bombardment, ASW

  Fast Transport

  Transport, Cargo, Air Transport, Support


         Replenishment


         Minelaying


         Minesweeping 


         Local minesweeping 

  Tanker

  Sub Patrol and Sub Transport


                       Sub Minelaying

  Amphibious


        Barge


        Landing Craft 


        PT Boat 

  Escort


        Midget Sub


        Midget Sub Carrier

  Multiple task forces.  When 3 or more Task Forces are present in the hex, this icon replaces the individual mission type icons.
4.2.2.6 Minefields Icon
A maximum of two Minefields Icons in a hex can be displayed on the Tactical Map: one for the Allied player and one for the Japanese player.  This limit is irrespective of the number of separate minefields which either side may have sown at that location.
The Minefields Icon is:

The color of the minefield indicates who laid the minefield.  A green-colored minefield indicates it was sown by the Americans whereas a tan colored minefield indicates a British minefield.
Friendly minefields are immediately displayed but enemy minefields are only displayed once they have been detected.  When displayed, the Minefields Icon is found in the upper left of the hex.
4.2.2.7 Flagged Events Icons
With the Second Official Update for AE, three new icons were introduced.  These new icons indicate, on the Tactical Map, certain events which occurred in the previous Turn Resolution phase.  The Flagged Event Icons are:

  A yellow crossed swords icon is placed in a hex where land combat occurred in the previous Ground Combat Phase.

  A red exclamation icon is placed in a base when either a partisan attack in the previous Logistics Phase occurred due to the garrison requirement not having been met, or the supply level is less than the minimum amount necessary to meet current operations

  A yellow exclamation icon is placed in a base to warn the player the supply level is between the minimum amount necessary to meet current operations and 2x the minimum amount
4.2.3 Off Map Areas
In full map full length campaign scenarios, the Allied player may make use of special movement to, from and between special map areas that lie on the edge of the Tactical Map.  These special areas, or “off map areas”, represent areas of the globe that are separate from the Pacific and Indian ocean areas represented on the Tactical Map itself.  There are 10 Allied bases in these off map areas which are out of bounds for the Japanese player.
Bases in the off-map areas may be connected to the Tactical Map by land and/or sea connections. Direct movement is possible between the Tactical Map and any off map area base, which is connected to the Tactical Map, or between two off map area bases which are connected to each other via a sea connection.
If an off-map area contains more than one base in the same area (e.g. Panama) air, land and naval units may move between them using normal movement, according to the normal movement rules.
Where a land connection exists between the Tactical Map and an off-map area base, strategic movement may be used to move land and air units between the two.  This movement works in the same way as normal strategic movement on the Tactical Map.
Where Off Map Maritime Links exist (see section 4.2.1.5) naval movement to, from or between off map areas is handled in a different way from normal Tactical Map naval movement.  This movement is called “off map naval movement” and is explained in section 6.2.10.
Not all off map bases connect to each other, nor do all off map bases have a direct connection with the Tactical Map.  Where a direct connection is lacking, the Allied player can only move units between these unconnected bases in a two-stage process.  Stage 1 is to move the units to an intermediate connected on map/off base which itself is connected to the final destination.  Stage 2 involves sending the units from the intermediate base to the final destination.
4.2.3.1 Transit Zones
Transit Zones are marked on the Tactical Map as shaded ocean hexes.  Task Forces can use naval movement to move directly between the Tactical Map and off map base where an Off Map Maritime Transport Link exists (see section 4.2.1.5).
Each off-map area is only connected to, at most, one Transit Zone on the Tactical Map as detailed in the following table.

Transit Zone	Map hex coordinates	Connected off-map areas
Indian Ocean	4,12 to 4,175	Cape Town, Mombasa
Red Sea / Arabian Sea	13,5 to 36,5	Aden, Abadan
Pacific Ocean	226,83 to 227,182	Panama (Balboa)
South Pacific Ocean	227,184 to 227,202	Port Stanley

Narrow blue lines drawn on the Main Map Display indicate the existence of sea connections between off-map areas, and between off-map areas and the Tactical Map.  These lines are drawn for illustrative purposes only and do not serve a physical function in naval movement.  The blue lines simply illustrate which areas are connected.
Distances between the Transit Zone hexes, and the off-map bases they are associated with are determined by using a “virtual” hex coordinate for the off-map base.  This “virtual” coordinate is not the actual or physical hex coordinate of the off-map base, but one that is only used for calculating the distance in hexes, between the off-map base and hexes on the Tactical Map.
The virtual hex coordinates of the off-map bases are as follows:

Off-map base	Virtual Hex Coordinates
Aden	7, -5
Abadan	23, -12
Balboa	274, 134
Port Stanley	278, 238
Cape Town	-60, 88
Mombasa	-18, 12

Negative values in the table represent a “virtual” hex which is not included within the boundaries of the Tactical Map.
4.2.3.2 Holding Boxes
Each sea connection between on-map and off-map areas has two holding boxes associated with it, one for each direction of movement.
While a Task Force is undergoing off-map movement, it is placed in a Holding Box so that it can still be accessed from the Main Map Display itself rather than only from the Task Force List.  A Task Force located in a Holding Box can be directly accessed by clicking on its Task Force icon.
While undergoing off-map movement in the Holding Box, Task Forces cannot interact with other Task Forces in the same Holding Box or other Task Forces, bases and items located anywhere else.  This means that while in the Holding Box, a Task Force should not be given any new redirection movement orders, or transfer ships.  Players who have attempted to do so have reported on the AE forum unintended and unpleasant consequences.
4.3 Hex Command Display
Below the Tactical Map is the Hex Command Display.  When a hex is selected by clicking on it, all units, bases, industry and other items located in that hex are displayed in the Hex Command Display.  The format of each Hex Command Display is the same but only the items specific to that hex are shown.
On the left side of the Hex Command Display are five buttons.  Clicking on each button from top to bottom shows all
    • Ships at Anchor
    • Air Units at the Base
    • Task Forces in the hex
    • Land Combat Units in the hex
    • Industry at the Base

The screen which is displayed after clicking on each of the above buttons brings up a screen similar to the screens discussed in chapter 5.
The arrows next to the show Ships at Anchor allow for the player to cycle through his bases.  The currently selected base name appears in yellow.
The arrows next to the Air Units, Task Force and Land Combat Units buttons allow the player to cycle through the respective units located in the hex.  Unlike the Tactical Map, there are different graphics to distinguish the different types of land units.  The currently selected unit appears in yellow next to the arrow.  When there are more than 13 units present, an additional arrow is available to allow for cycling through the unit icons.  In the above Pearl Harbor Hex Command Display, this additional arrow is to the left of the row of LCU’s.
There are never any arrows next to the Industry at Base button as there are fewer than 13 industry types in the game.
Along the top of the Hex Command Display is a summary of the current base assets and development level of the facilities.  The summary display in Pearl Harbors Hex Command Display discloses the following;
    • The Pearl Harbor port is currently developed to a level 7 capacity.  The number (5) is the Standard Potential Size (“SPS”) of the port.  Section 9.2.2 explains the SPS concept. There is no damage to the airfield facility, so the numbers are in white. These numbers would be in red if there was any damage. 
    • The Pearl Harbor airfield is currently developed to a level 10 capacity.  The SPS of the airfield is 7.   Section 9.2.2 explains the SPS concept.  There is no damage to the airfield facility, so the numbers are in white.
    • The Pearl Harbor depots currently hold 184,310 supply points and 610,600 fuel points.  If there is a shortage of supply or fuel points at the base to meet the respective demand, the numbers will be in red
    • There are 103 ships anchored and 317 aircraft
4.4 Jump Map
In the lower right corner of the Main Map Display, and below the Tactical Map, is found the Jump Map.
The Jump Map is a thumbnail map of the total playing area.  A small yellow framed box denotes the play area currently displayed on the Tactical Map.  If playing a limited length and limited map scenario, a larger red framed box appears in the Jump Map to denote the scenario boundaries.
Clicking on a spot in the Jump Map quickly center’s the Tactical Map on the corresponding spot.  Alternatively, the Tactical Map can be moved by use of the arrow keys on your keyboard or by dragging the mouse curser to the edges of the Tactical Map. 
4.5 Mouse Over
When the mouse curser is moved over an icon on the Tactical Map (excluding the Minefields and three Flagged Events icons a summary of information relating to the icon appears in a pop-up window.  Mouse Over displayed information on player owned icons is accurate but the information displayed on enemy icons is both more limited and the accuracy is dependent on the current Maximum Detection Level (see section 10) on that enemy icon.

Below is the Mouse Over information provided by pointing the mouse curser at a player owned Base Icon.  Detailed explanations of the information provided are found elsewhere in the manual.

4.6 Strategic Map
From the Main Map Display screen, the large Strategic Map can be accessed by either clicking on the Strategic Map button on the Menu Bar or by pressing the hotkey [Ctrl - Z].



This is essentially a larger view of the Jump Map.  Unlike the Jump Map, the Strategic Map has many filters along the top and left edges.  Click on a filter to toggle it on (green) or off (yellow).  Use of the filters is explained in sections 4.6.1-4.6.2.
To exit the Strategic Map press
    • the Exit arrow in the top left corner or
    • the Escape key or
    • click on the display
If all filters are toggled on, green dots (Allied) and red dots (Japanese) indicate the current locations on the Tactical Map of all bases, land and air units situated on land hexes, and task forces.  Please note that one filter from each of the Top and Left edges must be toggled on in order to display anything on the Strategic Map.
Appropriate use of these filters is a quick way to gain an overview of where the frontlines lie and where units belonging to particular Commands are located.
4.6.1 Strategic Map Top Edge Filters
The Top Edge filters list all the Headquarter Command units in the scenario.  Click on a filter to toggle it on (green) or off (yellow).



In scenario 1 there are 16 Allied Headquarter Command units, of which those with [R] after their name are restricted HQs. (see section 8.1.1 for the significance of restricted HQ units).
4.6.2 Strategic Map Left Edge Filters
The Left Edge filters allow for fine tuning which bases, and units are displayed on the Strategic Map.  The first three filters determine which side is displayed and the bottom four filters determine which type of unit is displayed.



Show All Units – both Allied and Japanese units are displayed.  Toggling this filter on automatically toggles on all the Top Edge filters.
Enemy Units – only enemy units are displayed.
Friendly Units – only friendly units are displayed.
Task Forces – current task force locations are displayed.
Bases – developed and dot bases are displayed.
LCUs – land combat units situated on a land hex are displayed.  Land combat units on ships are not displayed.
Groups – air units on an operational airfield are displayed.  Air units on ships are not displayed. 
5.0 Information Screens
From the Main Map Display screen, several first-tier information display screens can be accessed either by clicking on their buttons in the Menu Bar along the top of the screen or pressing the associated hotkey (see section 4.1).  Detailed information on the progress status of the war and the game’s database is provided in the following information display screens.
5.1 Intelligence Display Screen
The Intelligence Display Screen is the single most important war progress status screen in the game.  It is shown below. 



The displayed text provides a summary of:
    • air activity in the previous turn and earlier turns for both sides
    • current victory score status, available Political Points and Soviet participation status
    • breakdown of current victory points score allocated to both sides
