Clicking on the arrow buttons on the right-hand side of the Intelligence Display Screen opens up new second tier information screens which have their own associated hotkeys (see section 2.2.2).
5.1.1 Air Activity in the previous and earlier turns
These two columns show a summary of air activity (Sorties) for both sides in the previous and earlier turns.  The number next to “Today” refers to the previous turn and the number next to “Campaign” refers to the earlier turns.



Sorties – number of sorties flown.
Air-to-Air Losses – number of aircraft lost in air-to-air combat.
Destroyed on field – number of aircraft destroyed at an airfield or on a ship which sank.
Destroyed by Flak – number of aircraft destroyed by land based and ship based anti-aircraft guns.
Operational Losses – number of aircraft lost due to other causes.  Aircraft which crashed on the return flight, in takeoff and landing accidents, while undertaking training flights, or due to pilot errors, are all classified as operational losses.

5.1.2 Victory Score, Political Points and Soviet participation status Summary
Directly beneath the two columns which detail the previous turn air activity, is summary information on Victory Score, Political Points and Soviet status.  This information provides a snapshot of how well a player is doing.



**Major Victory** - this notification appears approximately a month after the start of the scenario and indicates which side is winning, and the victory level achieved, if the game finished this turn.  In this instance the notification isn’t available as the game has just started. When it does appear, it will appear underneath the Allied or Japanese air activity. Section 17.0 details how victory in the game is determined.
Manchukuo Garrison: Not shown, 12481/8000 – shows the current (12481) and the scenario required (8000) AV points of the Manchukuo garrison which the Japanese player is required to maintain to keep the Soviet units inactive.  This notification only appears to the Japanese player.  Section 8.6 discusses the Manchukuo Garrison rule.
Political Points: 150 – the current number of Political Points available to the player.  Chapter 11.0 discusses the use and consequences of Political Points
Japanese Score: 2119 – the current Japanese Victory Points score
Allied Score: 11530 – the current Allied Victory Points score
Soviets Not Active – indicates whether Soviet units are currently active or inactive
5.1.3 Breakdown of current Victory Points Score
The middle column breaks down the current Victory Point Score, by category, for both sides.  



Allied Bases Controlled: 665 – number of bases currently owned by the Allied player.
Allied Base Points: 11764 – current value of Allied bases, in victory points, that the Allied player owns.
Japanese Bases Controlled: 253 – number of bases currently owned by the Japanese player.
Japanese Base Points: 1439 – current value of Japanese bases, in victory points, that the Japanese player owns.
Allied Aircraft Points lost: 69 – current value, in victory points, awarded to the Japanese player for destroyed Allied aircraft.
Japanese Aircraft Points Lost: 59 – current value, in victory points, awarded to the Allied player for destroyed Japanese aircraft.
Allied Army Loss Points: 6 – current value, in victory points, awarded to the Japanese player for destroyed Allied land devices.
Japanese Army Loss Points: 0 – current value, in victory points, awarded to the Japanese player for destroyed Japanese land devices.
Allied Ships Sunk: 7 – number of Allied ships reported as sunk.
Points for Sunk Allied Ships: 605 – current value, in victory points, awarded to the Japanese player for reported sunk Allied ships.
Japanese Ships Sunk: 0 – number of Japanese ships reported as sunk.
Points for Sunk Japanese Ships: 0 – current value, in victory points, awarded to the Allied player for reported sunk Japanese ships.
Allied Strategic Losses: 0 – current value, in victory points, awarded to the Japanese player for damaged or destroyed Allied industrial facilities in Australia, Hawaii and North America.
Japanese Strategic Losses: 0 – current value, in victory points, awarded to the Allied player for damaged or destroyed Japanese industrial facilities in mainland Japan.
5.1.4 Menu Buttons on Intelligence Display Screen
The right-hand side of the Intelligence Display Screen has 15 Menu Buttons.  Each of these menu buttons has an associated alphanumeric hotkey.  Please note that the numeric hotkeys available in this screen are unique to the Intelligence Display Screen and that the alpha hotkeys available in the Intelligence Display Screen will access different screens if used on the Main Map Display Screen.  The full list of hotkeys is found in section 2.2.  Hovering the curser over a menu button also displays the hotkey. 14 of the menu buttons on this screen are common to both sides. The 15th button “Ship Withdrawal button” is only available to the Allied side. 



Clicking on a menu button accesses a new second tier information screen which has its own filters and capacity for re-sorting the order of information presented.  All these second-tier information screens are detailed in the following subsections.
5.1.4.1 Aircraft Losses Screen (Hotkey 1)
The Aircraft Losses Screen displays a breakdown of all aircraft losses.  It displays in greater detail the information summarized in section 5.1.1 above and which is also represented to the right of the scroll bar.  As different aircraft types generate different Victory Points, this screen does not tally with the information displayed in section 5.1.3 above. It summarizes the Victory Points gained by destroying aircraft.



The number in brackets above the yellow column headers is the sum total for that column.
The text column headers in yellow can be clicked on to change the sort order of that column.  Hovering the curser over the yellow column headers also generates a pop over which describes the column.
From left to right the 11 columns provide the following information.
Aircraft Type – each aircraft model which has had one or more planes destroyed to date in the scenario is listed here.  Models which have not yet suffered any losses in the scenario to date are not listed.
Today – previous turn losses from all causes.
Air-Air – previous turn losses from air to air combat.
Flak – previous turn losses from Flak (anti-aircraft guns).
Ground – previous turn losses that were destroyed either on the ground at an airfield or embarked on a ship sunk the previous turn.
Ops – previous turn operational losses.
Total – to date campaign losses from all causes.
Air-Air – to date campaign air to air losses.
Flak – to date flak campaign losses.
Ground – to date ground (airfield/sunk ships) campaign losses.
Ops – to date operational campaign losses.
Note that information on enemy aircraft losses is subject to Fog of War and therefore will not necessarily be accurate.  This is the reason why attentive readers will notice the difference in reported Japanese aircraft losses to the left of the scroll bar (42 losses) compared to the reported losses to the right of the scroll bar (41 losses).
To the right of the scroll bar the yellow “All Aircraft” text is a filter which if clicked on changes the information displayed on the screen from displaying both sides’ losses (the default “All Aircraft”) to only Japanese aircraft losses or only Allied aircraft losses.  Below this filter is the victory information described in section 5.1.3.
Clicking on the buttons at the bottom of the Aircraft Losses Screen from left to right will;
    • Open up the Destroyed Air Unit Screen (List Destroyed Air Groups) (see section 5.1.4.1.1)
    • Return to the Intelligence Display Screen (Back)
    • Return to the Main Map Display (Exit)



5.1.4.1.1 Destroyed Air units Screen
The Destroyed Air Units Screen lists air groups which have been destroyed.  It also provides the mechanism by which those destroyed air units, which meet the eligibility criteria for reactivation, may be selected by the player for a delayed re-entry into the game.  Clicking on the unit’s name will bring up unit info. Right clicking will bring up the reactivation cost and the location the unit will appear. 
Note; Air unit names in yellow are eligible for reactivation. Air units in white are not eligible.



The top line of the screen shows the number of destroyed air units and how many Political Points are currently available.
The second line from the top is a set of filters which, when selected, will show just that type of air unit. The abbreviations are used consistently throughout the manual and on game screens.  Details on aircraft types are found in section 7.0.1.
All Types – all aircraft types are displayed
F – Destroyed fighter units
FB – Destroyed fighter bomber units
NF – Destroyed night fighter units
DB – Destroyed dive bomber units
BM – Destroyed heavy, medium, light and attack bomber units
HB – Destroyed heavy bomber units
MB – Destroyed medium bomber units
LB – Destroyed light bomber units
AB – Destroyed attack bomber units
RC – Destroyed recon units
TR – Destroyed transport units
PA – Destroyed patrol units
FP – Destroyed float patrol units
FF – Destroyed float fighter units
TB – Destroyed torpedo bomber units

The third line has seven yellow column headers. Clicking on the yellow column headers sorts the column. The columns are;
Name – The name of the destroyed air unit.  Left clicking on the unit’s name brings up the aircraft model data screen which provides data on the aircraft model the unit was equipped with when destroyed.  Right clicking on the unit name gives the player the options for reactivating the air unit. 
Aircraft – The aircraft model which equipped the unit when it was destroyed.  If reactivated the unit will return with the same aircraft model.
Max Size – The maximum size of the unit when fully equipped.
Cost – The cost in Political Points to reactivate the unit.
Date - Date when the unit was destroyed.
Killed at/on – Location where the unit was destroyed, with “at” = airfield and “on” = ship.
Nationality – Nationality of the air unit.
On the right of the Destroyed Air Unit screen is a list of nationality filters which are toggled on (green) or off (yellow) by clicking on them.  Destroyed air units of that nationality are displayed if the filter is green or are not displayed if the filter is yellow.
5.1.4.2 Group Reinforcement Schedule (Hotkey 2)
The Group Reinforcement Schedule lists the arrival dates and locations of future reinforcement air units.



The top line is the standard set of aircraft type filters (see section 5.1.4.1.1) which are toggled on (green) or off (yellow) by clicking on them.  The aircraft type filters are used to display future reinforcement air units equipped with that type of aircraft.  The list of nationalities to the right of the scroll bar is also an additional set of filters.
All Types – all aircraft types are displayed.
F – Air units equipped with fighters.
FB – Air units equipped with fighter bombers.
NF – Air units equipped with night fighters.
DB – Air units equipped with dive bombers.
BM – Air units equipped with heavy, medium, light and attack bombers.
HB – Air units equipped with heavy bombers
MB – Air units equipped with medium bombers
LB – Air units equipped with light bombers
AB – Air units equipped with attack bombers
RC – Air units equipped with reconnaissance
TR - Air units equipped with transport planes
PA – Air units equipped with patrol planes
FP – Air units equipped with patrol planes
FF – Air units equipped with float fighters
TB – Air units equipped with torpedo bombers
 
The second line has five column headers in yellow. Clicking on the yellow column header sorts that column type.  
Unit Name - reinforcement air unit name.  Units which have an [S] next to their name cannot be reassigned to a different Headquarters.
Holding the curser over the yellow unit name generates a pop up which shows the following;
    • If the unit is scheduled to be withdrawn and the withdrawal date.
    • If the withdrawn unit returns and date of return.
    • The next scheduled aircraft model upgrade and earliest date for upgrade.
    • The date for unit resizing and new size.
The pop up on the yellow unit name also displays one or more of the following bracketed letters:
[S] Unit cannot be reassigned to a different Headquarters.
[D] Use the disband button on the air unit screen to affect the disbandment.
[W] Use the withdraw button on the air unit screen to affect the withdrawal.
Clicking on the yellow unit name brings up the aircraft model data screen and the number of airframes which will arrive with the unit.
Aircraft Type – the aircraft model which will equip the unit when it arrives.
Max A/C – maximum size of the unit.  Note most air units will arrive with less than their full complement of airframes.  The actual number of airframes which will arrive with the unit is seen by clicking on the yellow unit name.
Days Until Arrival – for units which are scheduled to arrive in less than 90 days, the number of days is given otherwise the arrival date is displayed.
Airfield of Arrival – the arrival location is listed here in white if arriving at an on-map airfield, in white with an asterisk if arriving at an off-map airfield, in grey if arriving on board a ship or in red if arriving at an on map airfield currently controlled by the enemy.  If the base is occupied by the enemy at the time the air unit is scheduled to arrive, the air unit will arrive later at its national base.
At the bottom of the screen are 4 buttons.  The “Turn of replacements/upgrades” button is only active if replacements and upgrades were toggled on at the Game Options Screen (section 2.5).  This button provides an easy correction if a mistake was made in toggling it on at the Game Options Screen.  Click on the “List withdrawal” button to access the Group Withdrawal Schedule Screen, which screen is also accessible from the Intelligence Display Screen and is explained in section 5.1.4.3.  Clicking on the “Back” and “Exit” buttons returns you to the Intelligence Display Screen and Main Map Display respectively.
5.1.4.3 Group Withdrawal Schedule (Hotkey A)
The Group Withdrawal Schedule lists the air units which are scheduled to be withdrawn by a certain date.  Failure to withdraw a unit by the due date will result in a penalty which is paid in Political Points.  This penalty is paid each day the unit remains past the due withdrawal date. 
This screen, see below, has a very similar layout and data presentation as found in the Group Reinforcement Schedule.  Both the top line and the list of nationalities to the right of the scroll bar are the same set of filters and function in exactly the same manner as those described in section 5.1.4.2.  The only difference in the filters is that instead of filtering for future reinforcing groups, here they filter groups scheduled to withdraw in the future. 



Holding the curser over the yellow unit name generates a pop up.  This pop up shows;
    • Withdrawal date (or days till withdrawal if less than 90).
    • If the unit can upgrade to a different aircraft model and date before withdrawing.
    • If the unit undergoes a renaming before withdrawing.
    • If the unit can resize and earliest date of the resize before withdrawing.
    • Whether after withdrawal the unit returns as a new unit.
The pop up on the yellow unit name also displays one or more of the following bracketed letters;
[S]unit cannot be reassigned to a different Headquarters.
[D]use the disband button on the air unit screen to effect withdrawal.
[W]use the withdraw button on the air unit screen to effect withdrawal.
The air unit name changes from yellow to red when only 7 or less days remain before the scheduled withdrawal date.
The bottom of the screen has three buttons whose function is exactly the same as that in the Group Reinforcement Schedule.  The different button here is the “List reinforcements” button which if clicked accesses the Group Reinforcement Schedule.
5.1.4.4 Aircraft Replacement Pool (Hotkey 3)
This is one of the most important and used information screens in the game.  From this screen the player has easy access to the following information on his side’s aircraft models:
    • performance data on each aircraft model
    • number of airframes in the pool for each aircraft model
    • monthly build rates for each aircraft model
    • availability dates for each aircraft model
    • the next aircraft model in the upgrade path
    • (for Japan only) the engine type used for each aircraft model
Each side has a slightly different Aircraft Replacement Pool. Both screens are shown below. 

5.1.4.4.1 Allied Aircraft Replacement Pool 



The top line has two filters in green which can be clicked on to toggle which aircraft models are displayed.  The default settings are “All Aircraft” and “With/Without Pools”.
All Aircraft (default) – all discontinued, current and future aircraft models are displayed. Clicking on this filter will bring up the other two filters, Active and R&D aircraft. Active aircraft are discontinued and active aircraft currently in play. R&D aircraft are those currently in development.
Aircraft with pools (filter option) – only aircraft models which have airframes in the pool are displayed.
Aircraft without pools (filter option) – only aircraft models which do not have any airframes in the pool are displayed.
Aircraft not building (filter option) – only aircraft with no current monthly build rate are displayed.
The second line from the top has the standard aircraft type filters.  To the right of the scroll bar is also the standard Nationality filters.  Use of both these filters is described in section 5.1.4.1.1.
Underneath the filters are 10 yellow column headings.  Clicking on the heading sorts the column.  From left to right the information shown is:
Name – aircraft model name.  Clicking on the model’s name opens the aircraft model data screen which lists performance data of that model.
Nation (Allies only) – the nation which builds that aircraft model.
In Pool – number of airframes currently in the pool.  Without airframes in the pool, air units equipped with this model cannot take replacements and other models can’t upgrade to this model. 
So Far Used – Total number of airframes, of this aircraft model, which have been dispatched to air units.  This number will be negative when the number of airframes returned to the pool, from air units which have ceased to operate the model, exceeds the number of airframes sent to air units.
This Turn – number of airframes built in the last turn (Replacement Rate/30 + Production Rate/30).
Month to Date – number of airframes built, for the current month, from both the Replacement and Production sides.
Replace Rate – monthly rate of airframes built off map.  Most Allied airframes come from this source which represents aircraft factories not located in the geographical area covered by the Main Map.  Scenarios which have been designed with Japanese production on have a zero Replace Rate for Japanese aircraft models.
Production Rate – monthly rate of airframes built in aircraft factories located on the Main Map Display.  This monthly rate can be rounded up or down slightly.  Full production is dependent on the availability of the necessary industrial inputs.  
Avail – date (in format year/month) when this aircraft model becomes available for building.  The Replacement Rate will kick in at the start of the month.  The Production Rate will kick in sometime during the month when the relevant aircraft factory has retooled
Upgrade to – aircraft models will usually, but not always, upgrade to another model within the same aircraft type.  If the aircraft model is scheduled to upgrade to a different aircraft model, the next model is listed in white, with “–rd” next to the name.  Where this applies the existing Production Rate will transfer to the indicated new model.  If there is no scheduled upgrade to a different aircraft model, the existing model name is listed in this column in grey.
5.1.4.4.2 Japanese Aircraft Replacement Pool 
This screen has only one significant difference with the Allied Aircraft Replacement Screen.  It replaces the “Nation” column with an “Engine Type” column.  All the other columns and filters work the same way as they do in the equivalent Allied screen.



Engine Type – this is the engine type used to build this aircraft model.  In scenarios designed with Japanese production on, engine type used is one of the key industrial inputs for the production of Japanese aircraft.  Allied aircraft production is not dependent on engine types.
As the Japanese player can rearrange their aircraft production to suit their priorities, Japanese aircraft models are not limited to the white/grey indicated model in the “Upgrade to” column.  For the Japanese player, the “Upgrade to” column indicates the scheduled on map aircraft factory research program.  Without player intervention the scheduled on map factory research production will automatically convert to production of the indicated aircraft model.  At a significant cost in time and supply the Japanese player can restructure this research program and consequently his future aircraft model production.  Overall, Japanese aircraft production is more complex than Allied aircraft production and a full understanding of section 13 is essential for good Japanese play.
5.1.4.5 List Top Pilots Screen (Hotkey 4)
This screen has two purposes.  The main purpose is to list pilots who have shot down enemy aircraft.  This adds flavor to the game and allows a player to follow the progress of any favored air aces.  The second purpose is to record pilot casualties which are separate from destroyed aircraft.


The top of the screen has nationality filters which can be toggled on (green) or off (yellow) by clicking on them.  The number in brackets after each nationality is the number of pilots of that nationality currently on the list.
Underneath the nationality filters are 10 columns.  Clicking on the yellow column header sorts the column.  From left to right the columns are;
Rank – current rank of the pilot.  This has no material effect on game play.
Name – pilot name.  Most pilot names are randomly generated but the pilot database includes historical pilots.
Exp - The current experience level of the pilot.
Fat – The current fatigue level of the pilot.
Mis – Number of missions flown to date by the pilot.
Kills – Number of aircraft destroyed in air combat by the pilot.
Fate – Current health status of pilot.  It can be one of the following:
    • “-“ Currently alive and available.
    • “MIA” = Missing In Action – there is a very small possibility that he may return.
    • “WIA” = Wounded In Action – will return if he recovers from his wounds before the end of the scenario.
    • “KIA” = Killed In Action – will not return.
Unit – Name of air unit the pilot is currently assigned to.  Clicking on the yellow unit name accesses the air unit display screen.
Nat – Nationality of the pilot.
Type – Aircraft model flown when the last kill was made.  
At the lower left bottom of the screen the total campaign and previous turn pilot casualties are listed. The lower right has the Back and Exit buttons.
5.1.4.6 Pilot Replacements Display (Hotkey 5)
This screen provides the player with general information on the 12 month pilot training cycle for his side.  All computer-generated pilots undertake a 12 month pilot training cycle.  Upon graduation each unnamed pilot is qualified for assignment to any aircraft type and aircraft model flown by their nationality.  See section 16.3 for details on pilot training.
Named historical pilots who reside in the game’s pilot database do not enter the game through this training cycle.



The five yellow column headers when clicked will sort the column.  From left to right the columns provide the following information;
Nationality – pilot nationality.
In Pool – number of currently available unnamed pilots in the pilot replacement pool.  In the above example there are 6770 unnamed US Navy pilots currently available in the pilot replacement pool for assignment to US Navy air units.
Added – number of new pilots added to the pilot replacement pool at the start of the month after graduating from the 12 month training cycle.
Experience – the national experience level of new graduates when added to the pilot replacement pool.  In the above example, US Navy pilots will graduate with an experience level of 40.  Note this is not necessarily the experience level individual pilots will have when assigned to an air unit.
Replacement – the monthly rate of unnamed pilots who commence month 1 of the 12 month training cycle.  In the above example, 600 US Navy pilots will enter the training cycle on 1 January 1942 and barring other factors these 600 will graduate on 1 January 1943.
While the pilot training cycle runs for 12 months, with the computer keeping track on a monthly basis of the details on student numbers and flying proficiency, in order to fit the information onto this screen the monthly details have been totaled into 4 columns (Months 1-3, 4-6, 7-9 and 10-12).  The four columns are displayed in the next 8 left to right sub-columns.
# - is the total number of student pilots in the sub-column.  In the above example there are 1750 US Navy pilots in the sub-column for months 1-3. On average 1/3 of the pilot trainees for months 10-12 will graduate at the start of the next month and be added to the pilot replacement pool.
Exp - The average experience level of the pilot trainees. 
Under certain circumstances (See section 16.3) it is possible for pilots to be accelerated by skipping the next month in the training cycle.  When this occurs, the player will receive a message indicating how many pilots have been accelerated and from which month.  A consequence of accelerated pilot training is that later in the game the player may notice columns being oversized compared to what 3 months of the replacement rate would provide, and conversely some columns will be undersized.  Over the course of the 12 month cycle, accelerated pilot training does not result in more graduating pilots.
At the bottom of the screen clicking on the “Back” and “Exit” buttons will return you to the Intelligence Display screen or to the Main Map Display respectively.  Clicking on the “Reserve pool” button accesses the various Named Pilot Reserve Pools which are shown in section 5.1.4.6.1 below.
5.1.4.6.1 Named Pilot Reserve Pool 
A random name is generated for pilots when they are assigned to an air unit.  Once named, a pilot can be placed in one of three additional pilot pools.  These three additional pools are;
    • Group Reserve -named pilots already assigned to an air unit but either currently inactive on the displayed pilot roster or in transit.
    • General Reserve -named pilots not assigned to any air unit. These pilots normally have some skills from either being released from an active squadron or from a training squadron.
    • TRACOM - Training Command. Only qualified highly experienced pilots not assigned to an air unit will be found in this pool.
All three named pilot reserve pools are accessed from the Pilot Reserve Pool button located at the bottom of the Pilot Replacement Pool screen. (See section 5.1.4.6)
The same screen layout format is used for the named pilot pools.  Clicking on the yellow “Reserve” (this is the default pool displayed) button on the top line will cycle through TRACOM, any (where all pilots in all 3 reserves are displayed), Group and back to Reserve.



The second line from the top is a set of filters which if clicked will toggle to on (green) or off (yellow) to display pilots who last flew;
Fighter – Fighters, fighter bombers, night fighters, float fighters.
Bomber – Dive bombers, heavy bombers, medium bombers, light bombers, attack bombers, torpedo bombers.
Patrol – Patrol planes, float planes.
Transport – Transport planes.
Recon – Reconnaissance planes.
The number in brackets next to each aircraft type filter is the total number of pilots in the pool of that aircraft type in the pool on display.
Underneath these filters is a set of nationality pilot filters which if clicked will toggle to display (green) or not display (yellow) pilots of that nationality.  The number in brackets next to each nationality filter is the total number of pilots in the pool of that nationality in the pool on display.
Underneath the nationality filters are nine yellow column headers.  Clicking the yellow column headers sorts the column.  From left to right these columns provide the following information;
Rank – Rank of the named pilot.  The rank adds flavor to the game as pilots are sometimes promoted.
Name – Name of pilot.  No distinction is made between computer generated pilots and historically named pilots.
Due – Date the historically named pilot is scheduled to arrive in the game.  The date format is year/month.
Mis – Number of missions flown by the pilot.
Kills – Number of enemy aircraft destroyed in air-air combat by the pilot.
Exp – Current experience level of the pilot.
Status – Current health status of the pilot where;
    • WIA means wounded in action and currently not available.
    • MIA means missing in action and currently not available.
    • OK means alive and available.
Group – indicates which of the three pools the pilot is currently located in where;
    • “Aircraft type” + “reserve” means the pilot is in the General Reserve Pool.
    • Air unit name means the pilot is in the Group Reserve Pool.
    • TRACOM means the pilot is in the TRACOM Pool.
Nat – Nationality of the pilot.
At the bottom of the General Reserve Pool display there is a single “Release pilot” button.  If instead the other pool screens are toggled on, additional options to release pilots are available.
Clicking on these buttons allows the player to transfer pilots on mass from one pool to another pool.  Pilot transfers are explained in 16.3 and in almost all instances they should only transfer between pool and air unit.
5.1.4.7 Sunk Ships Screen (Hotkey 6)
This screen lists ships of both sides which have been reported as sunk.  Ships are normally sunk as a result of combat, either directly in the naval battle itself or indirectly from damage sustained in battle. Ships which were building when their shipyard is captured by the enemy are also considered to be “sunk” and will appear on this screen.
Enemy ships which are reported to be sunk are subject to Fog of War and do not necessarily appear immediately on the list.  A delay of up to 60 days may result before an enemy ship which has been reported as having been sunk, appears on the Sunk Ships screen.  On occasion enemy ships which are listed as sunk will be reported as to be still in service and removed from the list.


The top line of the Sunk Ships screen is a standard set of ship type filters which are used in this and various other screens.  When the filter is on (green) ships of that type which have sunk will appear and conversely, they will not appear if the filter is off (yellow).  Clicking on the filters toggles them on/off.  A detailed explanation on ship types is found in section 20.1.2.
From left to right the ship type filters are:
All Ships – display all ships (default setting)
CV/CVL – aircraft carriers and light aircraft carriers
CVE – escort carriers
BB/BC – battleships and battlecruisers
CA/CL – heavy cruisers and light cruisers
DD – destroyers
DE – destroyer escorts
APD – destroyer transports
AP – troop transports
AK – cargo ships
AO – fleet oilers
TK – tankers
SS – submarines
Aux – auxiliary vessels
Mine – minelayers and minesweepers
Pat – patrol craft
LS – landing ships
LC – landing craft
The second line from the top is a set of five filters which are toggled on (green) or off (yellow) by clicking on them.  From left to right these filters are:
Japanese ships – displays sunk Japanese ships
Allied ships – displays sunk Allied ships
All Dates – displays ships sunk since the beginning of the scenario
Last Turn – Displays only ships which sank last turn
Last month – Displays only ships which sank in the current month
Underneath the filters are seven columns.  Clicking on the yellow column header sorts the column.
Type – Ship type.
Name – Name of ship.  Clicking on the yellow ship name accesses the ship data screen which displays the status of the ship at the time of sinking.
Points – Number of Victory Points awarded.  The amount awarded will vary depending on the cause of sinking.
Navy – Nationality of the ship.
Sunk Date – Date of sinking.
Sunk By – Main weapon responsible for the sinking.
Near – Location of sinking.  Note sunk “at” usually means the ship sank in port whereas “near” usually means the ship was at sea when sunk.
Clicking on the right bottom buttons “Back” and “Exit” returns you either to the Intelligence Display Screen or the Main Map Display respectively.
5.1.4.8 Ship Availability Screen (Hotkey 7)
The Ship Availability screen lists the future arriving historical ships which either were deployed to the Pacific War (Allied or Japan) or were under construction but never completed (Japan only).  The player only sees the ships which belong to his side.  The list will be empty on turn 1 if the Variable Reinforcement option was selected.
The format of the Ship Availability screen is slightly different for the Allied and Japanese player.  The expanded Japanese screen format reflects the capacity of Japan to influence the scheduled arrival date of its ship reinforcements. Section 13.0 details how the Japanese player can accelerate or halt individual ships.
5.1.4.8.1 Allied Ship Availability Screen
The Allied player has no control over his ship building program.  Allied ships arrive on their historical date and location.  The historical arrival date can be varied if the Variable Reinforcement Arrival option was selected (see section 2.4.10).



The top line of the screen shows the same standard set of ship type filters explained in section 5.1.4.7.
Underneath the ship type filters are four columns.  Clicking on the yellow column header sorts the column.  From left to right these columns show:
Type – Ship type.
Name – Name of ship.  Ship names with “II” at the end of the name (for example Yorktown II) are historical ships which were named after Allied ships sunk earlier in the war.  Ships with the “II” suffix can be renamed while they remain in the reinforcement list.  Once the ship arrives on the Main Map Display it can no longer be renamed.  To rename click on the yellow ship name in this column which accesses the ship data screen and then click on the yellow ship name at the top of the ship data screen. Then type in the ships new name.
ETA – Number of days before arrival if less than 90, otherwise the date of arrival.
Arrival Location– The scheduled arrival location for the ship.  An asterisk indicates the arrival location is off map.  Note: If the arrival location is captured by the enemy all scheduled ships still to arrive at that location are treated as destroyed “while under construction” and Victory Points are awarded to the enemy.
At the left hand bottom of the screen, 3 small boat pools are displayed (PT Boats, MGBs and Landing Craft).  The number next to each shows how many currently exist in the pool.  The numbers in brackets indicate (normal delay/building is halted) before being added to the pool.  If available in the pool, these small boats are purchased with supply points. (See section 6.2.16)
To the right of the scroll bar is a set of nationality filters.  They are green if toggled on and yellow if toggled off.  The default setting is to not display Soviet ships before Soviet activation.
Clicking on the bottom two buttons, “Back” and “Exit” return to the Intelligence Display Screen and the Main Map Display screen respectively.
5.1.4.8.2 Japanese Ship Availability Screen
Unlike the Allied player, the Japanese player has total control in managing their industrial production to meet the various demands of their war economy.  The Japanese player can choose to expend additional industrial output to accelerate his ship building program or conversely reduce the pace of his ship building program in order to conserve industrial output for use by other parts of the war economy. Section 13.0 details the complex task which is managing the Japanese war economy.



The layout of the Japanese Ship Availability screen is similar to the Allied screen.  The four important differences between the two screens are:
    • The addition of a Build Rate on the Japanese screen.
    • Above the small boat pools a set of buttons to control the pace of the ship building program.
    • For small boat pools, Japan has Midget Subs and Barges but no Landing Craft pools.
    • Instead of the nationality filters to the right of the scroll bar, a snapshot of the total industrial output is presented.

The Build Rate shows the current construction mode and the number of days under that mode for completion.  Clicking on the yellow build rate of a ship cycles through the normal, accelerated and halted completion modes.
Clicking on one of the Halt, Normal, Accelerate and Stop build buttons sets all ships on display to that completion mode.
To the right of the scroll bar the industrial production snapshot provides two numbers.  The first number shows how much is the production per turn.  Excluding Aircraft Engines and Aircraft Assembly, the number in brackets shows how much of that industrial output is in the global pool.  For aircraft engines and assembly, the number in brackets shows how much is allocated to R&D.
Clicking on the bottom right Back and Exit buttons returns to the Intelligence Display and Main Map Display screens respectively.
5.1.4.9 Ship Withdrawal Screen (Hotkey W) (Allied Player Only)
Only the Allied player is required to withdraw ships.  Some Allied ships need to be redeployed to other theatres and these are listed on the Ship Withdrawal Screen.  Players can choose to not withdraw the specified ship but not withdrawing by the due date results in a substantial daily penalty paid in lost Political Points.  Full details about the withdrawing of ships can be found in sections 6.9 and 11.1.2.



The top line of the screen shows the same standard set of ship type filters explained in section 5.1.4.7.
Clicking on the 7 yellow column headers sorts the column.  From left to right the columns show:
Type – Ship type.
Name – Name of ship to be withdrawn.  Clicking on the yellow ship name accesses the ship data screen.  The ship name will be in red if it remains in play past it’s withdraw date. 
Withdraw – Date withdrawal is due.
Days – Number of days remaining before the withdrawal date.
Cost – The amount of Political Points lost each day the ship remains in play beyond its withdrawal date.
Location – The current location or task force number of the ship.  Note, this is not the location required to withdraw the ship.
Return – The date on which the ship is scheduled to return. Not all ships will return after it withdraws.
To the right of the scroll bar is the standard nationality toggle filter set.
The bottom of the screen has 3 display switches which are toggled on (green) or off (yellow) by clicking on it.
All Withdrawals – All ships due to withdraw in the scenario.
Next Month – Ships due to withdraw in the next 30 days.
Due and Overdue – Ships due to withdraw soon or past their withdrawal date.
Clicking on the bottom right Back and Exit buttons returns to the Intelligence Display and Main Map Display screens respectively.
5.1.4.10 Ground Reinforcement Schedule (Hotkey 8)
The Ground Reinforcement Schedule Screen lists all the ground units which are due to arrive for that side before the scheduled end of the scenario.  The list will be empty on turn 1 if the variable reinforcement option was selected (see section 2.4.10).



The top line is a standard set of ground unit filters.  Click the filter to turn on (green) or off (yellow) that type of ground unit.  Full details on ground unit types are found in section 8.0.
From left to right the ground unit type filters are:
All Units – All ground unit types.
HQ Units – Only Headquarter units.  Includes the sub types Command, Corps, Amphibious, Naval and Air.
Infantry – Only infantry type units.
Armor – Only armor type units.
Artillery – Only artillery type units.  Includes the sub types AA (anti-aircraft), Art (artillery), CD (coastal defense).
Engineers – Only engineer type units.
The second line is a filter which if you left click will cycle forward (right clicking cycles in reverse) through the restriction status of the unit.
    • All units – Both restricted and unrestricted units are displayed (default setting).
    • [S]tatic attached – Permanently attached units are displayed.
    • [R]estricted attached – Permanently restricted units are displayed.
    • (R)estricted attached – Temporarily restricted units are displayed.
    • Unrestricted – Unrestricted units are displayed.
Underneath the top filters are 7 columns.  Clicking on the yellow column header sorts the column.  From left to right the columns show:
Type – Type of ground unit.  A “p” next to the type indicates the unit is air drop capable.
Name – Name of the ground unit.  Clicking on the yellow ground unit name accesses the unit data screen.  Hovering the curser over the yellow ground unit name generates a pop over which advises;
    • If the unit is scheduled to be withdrawn, the withdrawal date and whether it is withdrawn out of theatre.
    • Date the TOE can be upgraded.
    • Unit strength upon arrival in format (x/y) where x represents percentage of ready devices and y represents percentage of TOE.
    • If the ground unit is a Headquarters type, identifies the sub type (Command, Corps, Amphibious, Naval, or Air).

The mouse over on the yellow unit name also displays one or more of the following bracketed letters:
[S] Unit cannot be reassigned to a different Headquarters.
[R] Unit is permanently restricted.
(R) Unit is temporarily restricted. 
Attached to – Name of headquarters the ground unit is attached to. If the HQ name is in white, upon arrival the ground unit can be reattached to another HQ at the cost of paying Political Points. If the HQ name is in grey, no reattachment is possible.
Load cost – The troop and cargo space used by the unit for it to be fully loaded onto ships. 
Assault – The combat assault value of the unit.
Days Until Arrival – Days until arrival are shown if less than 90 otherwise the arrival date is shown.  Japanese players should pay particular attention to the arrival date as their ground units will arrive greatly below the strength disclosed by the pop over if insufficient weapons are in the pools to equip the unit completely up to the indicated arrival level (see section 13.7).
Base of Arrival – Location where ground unit will arrive.  An asterisk indicates an off map base.  The location is in red if the enemy currently occupies the arrival base (see section 16.6 for what happens if the location remains enemy controlled on the arrival date).
Section 8.0 explains in detail the operation of ground unit types including upgrades, TOE, withdrawal and reassignments to different Headquarters.
Underneath the columns is the standard set of nationality filters.  Click on the filter to turn it on (green) or off (yellow).
The bottom line has a filter to globally turn off replacement/upgrades.  This filter is only available if the player started the game with replacements/upgrades turned on (see section 2.5.7).
Clicking on the bottom right Back and Exit buttons returns to the Intelligence Display and Main Map Display screens respectively.
5.1.4.11 Ground Withdrawal Screen (Hotkey G)
The Ground Withdrawal Screen lists the ground units which are scheduled to be withdrawn before the scenario concludes. 



This screen has almost the same format as the Ground Reinforcement Schedule Screen which is explained in section 5.1.4.10 above.  The only differences between the two screens are: 
    • The Ground Withdrawal screen substitutes the Days Until Arrival column found on the Ground Reinforcement Schedule screen with a Withdraw in days column which shows the number of days (if less than 90) before the unit is due to withdraw, otherwise the withdrawal date.
    • The Ground Withdrawal screen substitutes the Base of Arrival column with a ‘Delay in days’ column.  The Delay column indicates whether the ground unit is already in play (“On map”) or whether it is a future scheduled reinforcement unit in which case the arrival date (or days till arrival if less than 90) is shown.
    • On the Ground Withdrawal screen, an * at the end of the unit name indicates the unit loses devices when withdrawn.
Clicking on the bottom right Back and Exit buttons returns to the Intelligence Display and Main Map Display screens respectively.

5.1.4.12 Ground Units Destroyed Screen (Hotkey K)
The Ground Units Destroyed Screen lists all of the ground units which have been destroyed up to the current date. 



This screen has almost the same format as the Ground Reinforcement Schedule Screen which is explained in section 5.1.4.10 above. The only differences between the two screens are:
    • The Ground Unit Destroyed Screen substitutes the Load Cost column for a Nation column. If you click on this button it will sort the nations with Ground Unit Losses.
    • The Ground Unit Destroyed Screen substitutes the ‘Days Until Arrival’ column with the ‘Date Killed’ column. Clicking on this button sorts the dates units were killed. Also adds a Formation Type column which shows the Formation Type of the unit. Clicking on this button sorts the column.
    • The Ground Unit Destroyed Screen substitutes the Base of Arrival column with a Cost to Recall column. Clicking on the button will sort the column by No Recall, Not Defined and a Political Points cost to reactivate the unit.
The first line across the top shows:
    • Destroyed LCU’s- The number of Land Combat Units which have been destroyed to date.
    • Political Points- The number of available Political Points.
The second line has one new sorting button. The Suppress No Recall button. Clicking on this button sorts LCU’s by All Units Destroyed or Not Defined units. Not Defined units are the LCU’s which can be bought back with Political Points. 
Clicking on the bottom right Back and Exit buttons returns to the Intelligence Display and Main Map Display screens respectively.
5.1.4.13 Industry/Resource Availability Screen (Hotkey 9)
The Industry/Resource Availability screen lists the bases where industrial facilities exist.  It also provides a snapshot of the industrial output pools.  More detailed dynamic information on the war economy and the capacity to manage it from a single screen is found in the Industry Management screen (see section 5.3) which was added to the game in the official update #2 released in December 2009.
The Allied and Japanese Industry/Resource Availability screens are the same in format, but they show slightly different information as some of the industrial outputs are unique to only one side.  The different operations of the respective war economies are discussed at length in section 13.0.
5.1.4.13.1 Allied Industry/Resource Availability Screen
To the left of the scroll bar the Allied Industry/Resource Availability screen is the same as the Japanese screen.  To the right of the scroll bar, the Allied screen displays information on the one unique Allied industry production output (Atomic Bombs) but does not display information on the five industry outputs which are unique to Japan (see section 5.1.4.13.2 for the Japanese screen). 


The top of the screen is a set of 15 industry facility toggle filters.  Click on the filter to toggle on (green) or off (yellow), the display of locations with that industrial facility.
From left to right the industry facility filters are:
All Industry – All industry facilities are displayed (default setting).
Manpower – Manpower centers.
HI – Heavy industry centers.
LI – Light industry centers.
Refinery – Refinery centers.
Oil – Oil centers.
Resources – Resource centers.
Armament – Armament factories.  This is a unique Japanese industrial facility.
Vehicle – Vehicle factories.  This is a unique Japanese industrial facility.
Engines – Engine factories.  This is a unique Japanese industrial facility.
Air – Aircraft assembly factories. This is a unique Japanese industrial facility.
RD Air – R&D aircraft assembly factories. This is a unique Japanese industrial facility.
Nav SY – Naval shipyards.  This is a unique Japanese industrial facility. 
Mer SY – Merchant shipyards.  This is a unique Japanese industrial facility.
Rep SY – Repair shipyards
Underneath the filters are three columns.  Clicking on the yellow column header sorts the column.
From left to right the columns display:
Location – The base name appears for each industry output facility which is located there.  Clicking on the yellow location name takes you to the Main Map Display centered on the base.
Type – Industry output facility.  The number in brackets is the amount currently held in storage of that output at the base.
Amount – Current number of undamaged industry output centers.  The number in brackets is the current number of damaged industry output centers.
To the right of the scroll bar is a snapshot of the industry output pools.  The number shown is the total produced last turn for that industry output.  The number in brackets is the pool for each industry output except for Aircraft Assembly whose number in brackets represents the number of R&D aircraft factories.  Note some industry output types are stored locally in individual bases or onboard ships, and other industry output types are stored globally.  Full details are found in section 13.0.
Clicking on the bottom right Back and Exit buttons returns to the Intelligence Display and Main Map Display screens respectively.
5.1.4.13.2 Japanese Industry/Resource Availability Screen
This screen operates in the same manner as the Allied Industry Availability Screen.  The difference in the two screens is entirely due to the five unique Japanese industry outputs whose totals are found to the right of the scroll bar as shown below.



The five unique Japanese industry outputs are:
    • Naval Shipyard
    • Merchant Shipyard
    • Armament
    • Vehicles
    • Aircraft Engines

The two shipyards generate points for the building of ships and it is therefore usual to find their output pools to be consistently low. The armament and vehicle output pools tend to fluctuate depending on the demand for land unit replacements on arrival.  The number in brackets for aircraft engines is the number of engine factories currently tooled to produce engines not yet in production.
Clicking on the bottom right Back and Exit buttons returns to the Intelligence Display and Main Map Display screens respectively.
5.1.4.14 Industry/Troops/Resource Pool Screen (Hotkey 0)
The Industry/Troops/Resource Pool screen is somewhat inaccurately named.  A more accurate name for it would be the Devices Pool screen.  This is because the screen lists the various game devices which are produced by the war economy to equip units, primarily land units but also a couple of other non-land items used by non-land units.
The screen format is almost identical for both sides however the information displayed reflects the different war economy outputs. The only format difference in the two screens is that the Allied screen provides information on the atomic bomb pool and the Japanese screen provides information on the aircraft engines pools. The Allied screen is provided below.



The top line of the screen is a filter.  A left click on the filter cycles through the following displays:
    • With/Without Pools – all devices are displayed (default setting).
    • With Pools – only devices with some items in the pool are displayed.
    • Without Pools – only devices with no items in the pool are displayed.
    • With Upgrades – only devices with a scheduled upgrade are displayed.
    • Not building – only devices with no items produced this month to date are displayed.
Right clicking on the filter cycles the displays in reverse order.
The second and third lines are a set of device type filters.  Clicking on the filter turns it on (green) or off (yellow).  From left to right these filters display:
GP Bomb – Atomic bomb (appears on Allied screen only).
Srch Rdr – Search radar
AAA Gun – Anti aircraft weapons
Fac/Mach – Factory/Machine.  The Allied screen displays only heavy industry, light industry and refinery factories.  The Japanese screen, in addition to these three factories, also displays naval shipyards, merchant shipyards, armament assembly and vehicle assembly.
DP Gn – Dual purpose guns
Nvl Gn – Naval guns
Army Gn – Army weapons
Mine – Naval mines.  Note there are no land mines in the game.
AFV – Armored Fighting Vehicles.  Includes tanks, tank destroyers, armored cars, self-propelled assault guns, etc.
Squad – Squads
Engr – Engineers
Vehicle – Non-armored fighting vehicles such as engineer vehicles and motorized support.
Air Engine – Aircraft engines (appears on Japanese screen only.)
All Devices – All devices are displayed (default setting).
Underneath the device type filters are 8 columns.  Clicking on the yellow column header sorts the column.  From left to right these columns show:
Name – Name in white indicates the device has been or is currently available for production.  If the name is in grey, the device is not yet available for production.
In Pool – Number available for distribution to units.
Used So far – Total number distributed to date to units.  The number will be negative if more devices have been returned to the pool than have been distributed.
Produced Last Turn – Number produced last turn (build rate/30).
Produced This Month – Number produced in the current month.
Build Rate – Monthly build rate for the device.  The number produced in a month can vary slightly above or below the build rate due to rounding.  Note the Build Rate is only relevant for the Allied player or in scenarios where production is not on for the Japanese player.  Where the Japanese player has control over his production, devices (excluding mines which have a fixed Build Rate) are built as required subject to the availability of the necessary industry outputs for that device.
Available From Till – Devices do not start to be produced before their start date (format is year/month).  An * next to the start date indicates the device upgrades to another device.  Devices which have no end date continue in production until the end of the scenario.  Devices with an end date (format is year/month) continue to be produced until the end of the month.
Stockpiled – Clicking on the yellow “N” or “Y” determines whether the device will be distributed to units.  The yellow “N” indicates the device will be distributed to units which have replacements toggled on.  The yellow “Y” indicates the device is stockpiled and will not be distributed to units even if they have replacements toggled on.
At the bottom of the screen is the standard set of nationality filters.  Clicking on the filter turns it on (green) or off (yellow).
To the right of the scroll bar are the industry output pools.  The difference between the Allied and Japanese screens is explained in sections 5.1.4.13.1 - 5.1.4.13.2 above).  Underneath the industry output pools are two buttons.  A single click on the “Stockpile devices on the list” or “No stockpiling of devices on the list” button will set all the yellow toggles in the “Stockpiled” column to “Y” or “N” respectively.
Clicking on the bottom right Back and Exit buttons returns to the Intelligence Display and Main Map Display screens respectively.
5.1.4.15 Aircraft/Engine Production Pool Screen (Hotkey E) (Japanese Player Only)
The Aircraft/Engine Production Pool screen is only available to the Japanese player.  This screen lists Japanese aircraft currently available for production and engines currently in production (white) or yet to enter production (grey).



Unlike other information screens, there are no separate filters on this screen.  However, a right-click on the yellow “Used” or “Avail Year” column headers will change the column displayed information to “Need” and “End Date” respectively.  Left-click on the yellow column headers also does the usual sorting.
From left to right (including the right-click display option) the 10 columns show the following information:
Name – names of currently available aircraft and engines in production are displayed in white.  Engines not yet in production are in grey.
In Pool – Number of aircraft or engines in the pool.
Used – Total number of aircraft and engines produced to date.
Need – The currently estimated number required to meet future air unit reinforcements or upgrades.
Production Last Turn – Number produced last turn.
Production This Month – Number produced in the current month.
Rate – The current number of undamaged and (damaged) factories tooled for this aircraft/engine.
Avail Year – Production available from year/month.
End Date – Fixed production “ends” at the end of the indicated year/month. However in scenarios where production is on for the Japanese player this information is meaningless because production is not fixed, the player determines which aircraft and engines he will produce and for how long.
Engine – Indicates which engine is used by the named aircraft.
To the right of the scroll bar the total aircraft engine, and aircraft assembly factories are shown.  The number in brackets next to these totals is the current number of R&D factories.
Clicking on the bottom right Back and Exit buttons returns to the Intelligence Display and Main Map Display screens respectively.
5.2 List all Bases, Units and Task Force Screens
From the Menu Bar at the top of the Main Map Display (or clicking their hotkey) there are six first tier information display screens which provide an overview of
    • All friendly bases. Some limited enemy base information also (see section 5.2.1) (Hot Key B)
    • Land based air units (see section 5.2.2) (Hot Key A)
    • Naval air units (see section 5.2.3) (Hot Key N)
    • Ground units (see section 5.2.4) ( Hot Key G)
    • Active ships (see section 5.2.5) ( Hot Key S)
    • Task forces (see section 5.2.6) ( Hot Key T)
These six information display screens provide an overview of where assets are located and their current war fighting capabilities.  Greater detail is available when accessing their second-tier data screens which are explained in the sections below. 
5.2.1 List all Bases Screen (Hotkey B)
Two different screens can be toggled on to show different information regarding all bases.  Full information on friendly controlled bases together with limited information on enemy controlled bases is displayed.


The top line of the screen consists of base filters:
    • All Bases – Both friendly and enemy controlled bases are displayed.
    • Non-base – Dot bases or Beaches (see section 9.2.1.1).
    • Base – Bases with both airfield and port facilities.
    • Airfields – Bases with only airfield facilities.
    • Enemy – Enemy controlled bases.
These filters are on if green and are off if yellow.  The default setting is for information on friendly controlled bases only to be displayed thus the player needs to click on the “Enemy” toggle to display information on enemy controlled bases.
To the right of the base filters is the yellow “Show Resource and Garrison Data” toggle.  Click on this toggle to replace 4 of the default information columns with 4 these information columns:
    • Aviation Support is replaced by Resources.
    • Aviation Support Required is replaced by Oil.
    • Ships At Anchor is replaced by Garrison.
    • Total Aircraft is replaced by Garrison Required.
The second line has a single filter.  Left click on the yellow “Showing all levels” filter to cycle through these displays:
    • Showing all levels – all levels are shown (default).
    • Showing minimal levels – bases with insufficient or critical supplies, aviation support or garrison levels.
    • Showing low levels – bases with critical supplies, aviation support or garrison levels.
Right click on the filter to cycle through the displays in reverse order.
Including the additional information columns which are accessed by clicking on the top line toggle, the List all Bases screen provides 16 information columns.  Clicking on the yellow column header sorts the column.  From left to right these columns are (default set first):
Type – Type of base where the distinction is made between “Base”, which is a location with both port and airfield facilities (or the potential to build them), and “AF” which is a location with only airfield facilities (or the potential to build them).
Name – Friendly controlled bases are in yellow, enemy controlled bases are in red. Clicking on the yellow name accesses the Base Information screen (see section 9.1).
Fuel – Amount of fuel currently stored at the base.
Supplies – Amount of supplies currently stored at the base.  The amount will be shown in orange if an insufficient level (<2x supplies required) of supplies are stored.  The amount will be shown in red if a critical level (<supplies required) of supplies are stored.
Supplies Required – Estimated amount of supplies required to sustain current operations for 30 days.
Aviation Support – Amount of aviation support present.  The amount will be shown in red if a critical level (<aviation support required) exists.
Aviation Support Required – Amount of aviation support required to efficiently service the current number of aircraft stationed at the airfield.
Port – Size of port.  If a “b” is present, engineers are currently increasing the size of the port.  An “*” present indicates friendly mines are present.  An “^” present indicates an enemy minefield has been detected.
Airfield – Size of airfield.  If “b” is present, engineers are currently increasing the size of the port.  An “*” present indicates the airfield is overstacked.
Ships at Anchor – Number of ships disbanded in the port.
Total Aircraft – Number of aircraft stationed at the airfield.
Location – Geographical coordinates of the base.  Clicking on the yellow coordinates centers the map at the location of the base.
(Additional columns)
Resources – Amount of resources currently stored at the base.
Oil – Amount of oil currently stored at the base.
Garrison – Aggregated amount of Assault Value from ground units currently located at the base.
Garrison Required – Garrison level required to be maintained at the base.  The level is measured in terms of Assault Value.
Below the information columns there are 13 buttons which set all displayed bases, with a single click, to the indicated parameter.  Hovering the curser over a button brings up a popup which indicates the function of the button.  The player can therefore, with a single click, set all displayed bases to:
    • Start or stop industry repairs.
    • Reset back to zero any additional supply demand set at the Base Data screen.
    • Toggle on/off stockpiling.
    • Toggle on/off port construction only.
    • Toggle on/off fort construction only.
    • Toggle on/off airfield construction only.
    • Toggle on/off all port, fort and airfield construction.
Clicking on either of the bottom right Back and Exit buttons returns to the Main Map Display screen.
5.2.2 List all Land Based Air Units Screen (Hotkey A)
This screen provides an overview of all land based air units which are currently located at an airfield and allows easy access to each unit’s data screen where various actions are undertaken (see section 7.0).  Land based air units which are currently being transported by ship to an overseas airfield are not found on this screen but on the List all Naval Air Units screen which is explained in section 5.2.3 below.



The top line of the screen shows the total of aircraft in all land based air units (4373) which are displayed on the screen.  This total is broken down into Ready aircraft (4130), Not ready aircraft (192) and aircraft in group reserve (51).
The second line from the top has the standard aircraft type filters which are described in section 5.1.4.1.1.  The third line is the standard nationality filters.
In the top right hand corner there are two yellow filters.  Clicking the yellow “Show Group Stats” filter (this is the default setting) toggles the screen from showing 4 columns with pilot information (Num, Exp, Fat, Morale) to a screen with 4 columns showing group information on aircraft losses (Kills, Ops, Cmt, Grd) with the yellow toggle now reading “Show Pilot Stats”.  Clicking the yellow filter, underneath, cycles between:
    • Show day & night groups – All land based air units are displayed (default setting).
    • Showing day groups – Only land based air units set to day operations are displayed.
    • Showing night groups – Only land based air units set to night operations are displayed.

Below the aircraft type and nationality filters are 15 default plus 4 filter columns.  Clicking on the yellow column headers sorts the column.  Columns with white headers cannot be sorted.
From left to right these columns are (default set first):
Unit Name - Name of air unit.  The name will be in red if the unit is 7 days or less away from its scheduled withdrawal date.  Units which have [S] next to their name cannot be reassigned to a different Headquarters. Units with “#” next to their name are scheduled to be withdrawn. 
Hovering the curser over the yellow unit name generates a popup which advises:
    • If the unit is scheduled to be withdrawn and the withdrawal date.
    • If withdrawn, whether the unit returns and date of return.
    • Next scheduled aircraft model upgrade and earliest date for upgrade.
    • Date for unit resizing and new size.

The popup on the yellow unit name also displays one or more of the following bracketed letters:
[S] Unit cannot be reassigned to a different Headquarters.
[D] Use the disband button on the air unit screen to effect the withdrawal.
[W] Use the withdraw button on the air unit screen to effect the withdrawal.
Section 7.0 explains in detail the operation of air unit upgrades, resizing, withdrawal and reassignments to different Headquarters.
Clicking on the yellow unit name brings up the aircraft model data screen.
Aircraft Model – Current aircraft model which equips the unit.  Air units cannot be equipped with multiple aircraft models at the same time.
Upgd – Shows the unit upgrade status, where “Y” indicates the unit is set to automatically upgrade without player intervention to the next scheduled aircraft model and “N” indicates it requires player intervention to upgrade to a different aircraft model. 
Ready – Number of aircraft ready for air operations.
N/Rdy – Number of aircraft not ready for air operations due to repairs or maintenance requirements.
Reserve – Number of aircraft in group reserve.
Repls – Shows the unit airframe replacement status, where “Y” indicates the unit is set to automatically receive airframe replacements and “N” indicates airframe replacements require player intervention.
Pilot Num – Number of active pilots on the group roster.  Inactive pilots also on the roster are not included in this column.
Pilot Exp – Average experience level of active pilots on the roster.
Pilot Fat – Average fatigue level of active pilots on the roster.
Pilot Morale – Average morale level of active pilots on the roster.
Pilot Mode – Shows the air unit pilot replacement status, where “A” indicates the unit is set to automatically receive replacement pilots based on the experience level set in the air unit data screen and “M” indicates the unit only receives replacement pilots after player intervention.
Mission – Current primary mission set for the unit.  An “*” indicates the unit is undertaking multiple tasks.  The mission will be in blue if the unit is tasked to operate at night.
Patrol – Percentage level of unit set to ASW, naval search, train or rest. 
Location – Current location of the unit.  Clicking the yellow location centers the Main Map Display on that location.
(Additional columns)
Group Kills – Number of enemy aircraft shot down by the unit.
Group Ops – Number of operational losses sustained by the unit.
Group Cmt – Number of unit aircraft shot down by the enemy.
Group Grd – Number of unit aircraft destroyed while on the ground.
Below the information columns there are 8 buttons which set all displayed air units, with a single click, to the indicated parameter.  Hovering the curser over a button brings up a popup which indicates the function of the button.  The player can therefore, with a single click, set all displayed air units to:
    • Allow or disallow automatic pilot replacements.
    • Allow or disallow automatic upgrade to the next scheduled aircraft model.
    • Allow or disallow automatic airframe replacements.
    • Stand down air units.
    • Reactivate stood down air units allowing the unit commander to select mission and status.
Clicking on either of the bottom right Back and Exit buttons returns to the Main Map Display screen.
5.2.3 List All Naval Based Air Units Screen (Hotkey N)
This screen has exactly the same format of the List all Land Based Air units screen which is explained in section 5.2.2 above.  The differences between the two screens are:
    • Lists aircraft which operate from ships or which are loaded temporarily on a ship for transfer to an overseas airfield.
    • The aircraft type filters at the top of the screen do not include filters for heavy bombers, medium bombers or attack bombers as these aircraft types were not designed to operate from ships.
The most common aircraft types which will be shown on this screen are fighters, dive bombers and torpedo bombers stationed on aircraft carriers and floatplanes stationed on cruisers.  Of these aircraft types, it will be the carrier capable aircraft models which will be present as only they are capable of operating from aircraft carriers.
5.2.4 List All Ground Units Screen (Hotkey G)
This screen provides an overview of all ground units which are currently located either on the Tactical Map or at an off-map base.  From this screen there is easy access to each ground unit’s information screen where various actions are undertaken (see section 8.0).



The top line is the same standard set of ground unit filters which are described in section 5.1.4.10 above.
The second line contains two different filter sets.  The first set is the single filter “All units” which if left-clicked will cycle forward (right clicking cycles in reverse) through the restriction status of the unit.
    • All units – Both restricted and unrestricted units are displayed (default setting).
    • [S]tatic attached - Unit can’t be reassigned to a different Headquarters.
    • [R]estricted attached - Unit is permanently Restricted.
    • (R)estricted attached - Unit is temporarily Restricted.
    • Unrestricted.

The second filter set on the second and third lines is the standard nationality filters.
In the top right hand corner there is a single yellow filter.  Clicking the yellow “Show Soft” filter will toggle the screen to replace the R, U, OpMode, Pack, Combat, Target information columns of the default display setting with new Xp, Mor, Fat, Dis, TOE, %TOE information columns.  The filter will show “Show Hard” when the filtered information columns are displayed.
Below the filters, including the toggled columns there are 18 information columns.  Clicking on the yellow column headers sorts the column.  From left to right these columns (default columns first) are:
Type – type of ground unit.  A “p” next to the type indicates the unit is air drop capable.  An “*” next to the type indicates this is a sub unit of a “Parent” unit which can be created by combining all the sub units after they all arrive in the same location. If an “a”, “n”, “c”, “m” or “y” is next to an HQ type it indicates it is an air, naval, corps, amphibious or army HQ, respectively.
Name – name of the ground unit.  Clicking on the yellow ground unit name accesses the unit data screen.  Hovering the curser over the yellow ground unit name generates a popup which advises;
    • If the unit is scheduled to be withdrawn, the withdrawal date and whether it is withdrawn out of theatre.
    • Date the TOE can be upgraded.
    • Unit strength in format (x/y) where x represents percentage of ready devices and y represents percentage of TOE.
    • If the ground unit is a Headquarters type, identifies the sub type (Command, Air, Corps, Amphibious, Naval, Air).
The popup on the yellow unit name also displays one or more of the following bracketed letters:
[S] - unit cannot be reassigned to a different Headquarters
[R] - unit is permanently restricted.
(R) - unit is temporarily restricted.
Attached to – Name of headquarters the ground unit is attached to.  Where the HQ name is in white the ground unit can be reattached to another HQ.  Where the HQ name is in grey, no reattachment to a different HQ is possible.
Load cost – The total troop and cargo space used by the unit for it to be fully loaded onto ships.  A load cost of “Static” means the unit cannot be moved from its present location.
AV - The current combat assault value of the unit.
Location – Current location of the ground unit.  If the unit is currently not at a base but is out in the field, the x, y hex coordinates are shown instead.  If the unit is currently on board ships, the task force number which is transporting the unit is shown.
R – Shows the unit device replacement status, where “Y” indicates the unit is set to receive replacement devices automatically and “N” indicates the unit has turned off taking device replacements.  Clicking on the yellow indicator toggles the replacement status.  Note replacement devices will only be received if they are available in the pools and the pools are not set to stockpile (see section 5.1.4.14).
U – Shows the unit upgrade status, where “Y” indicates the unit is set to upgrade automatically and “N” indicates the unit has turned off upgrades.  Clicking on the yellow indicator toggles the upgrade status.  Note devices will only be upgraded if they are available in the pools and the pools are not set to stockpile (see section 5.1.4.14).
OpMode – Shows the Operations Mode of the unit. Left-clicking on the yellow indicator cycles through the available Operations Mode in the following order: Combat, Move, Strat, Reserve, Rest.  Right-clicking cycles through in the reverse order.
Pack – Shows how many days remain before the unit can shift to, or from, strategic operations mode.
Combat – Shows the Combat Mode of the unit (see section 8.2.1.2).  Left-clicking on the yellow indicator cycles through the available Combat Mode in the following order: Defend, Bombard, Deliberate, Shock.  Right-clicking cycles through in the reverse order.
Target – Shows the Future Objective of the unit and how far advanced it is towards it (see section 8.2.1.4).
Xp – Experience level of the ground unit.
Mor – Morale level of the ground unit.
Fat – Fatigue level of the ground unit.
Dis – Disruption level of the ground unit.
TOE – Number of days before TOE upgrade becomes available.
%TOE – Current ready devices as a percentage of unit TOE.
Below the information columns there are 6 buttons which set all displayed ground units, with a single click, to the indicated parameter.  Hovering the curser over a button brings up a popup which indicates the function of the button.  The player can therefore, with a single click, set all displayed ground units to
    • Allow or disallow automatic upgrades (see section 16.4.1).
    • Allow or disallow automatic device replacements (see section 16.4).
    • Set Combat Mode (click on yellow toggle next to button) (see section 8.2.1.2).
    • Set Operations Mode (click on yellow toggle next to button) (see section 8.2.1.1).
Note setting a specific Combat or Operations Mode using the global button at the bottom of the screen will only take effect on those ground units which are currently eligible to adopt that specific Combat or Operations Mode.
Clicking on either of the bottom right Back and Exit buttons returns to the Main Map Display screen.
5.2.5 List all Active Ships Screen (Hotkey S)
This screen provides an overview of all currently active ships units which are located either on the Tactical Map or at an off map base.  From this screen there is easy access to each ships data screen where various actions are undertaken.
Ships which have been taken off line to undertake repairs or upgrades are not currently active, and therefore will not appear on this list by default.  They can, however, be displayed by clicking on the “Show Ships under repair” button at the bottom of the screen.
It is important to understand that unlike air and ground units, ships are not units which operate independently.  All ships are either disbanded in a port (anchored) or in a task force and operate within the parameters set by those two conditions.



The top line of the screen shows the same standard set of ship type filters which are explained in section 5.1.4.7 above.  To the right of the scroll bar is the standard set of nationality filters.
Below the ship type filters there are 14 information columns.  Clicking on the yellow column headers sorts the column.  From left to right the columns provide the following information.
Type – Ship type.
Name – Ship name.  Clicking on the yellow ship name accesses the ship data screen.  Hovering the curser over the yellow ship name also generates a popup which advises of;
    • The ship type.
    • If the ship is scheduled to be withdrawn and the withdrawal date.
    • If the ship is scheduled to return after first being withdrawn and the return date.
    • The upgrade date.
Endurance – Shows the current ship’s Endurance.  The Endurance number shown is the number of nautical miles the ship can travel at its cruising speed (see section 6.2.3).
Speed – The current maximum speed in knots.
Ops – The Operational Points used in this 12 hour phase (see section 6.2.14).
Cap – For combat ships this shows the aircraft carrying capacity of the ship.  For non-combat ships it shows the total troop, cargo and bulk liquid carrying capacity.
Damage Sys – Current systems damage level.  The severity of the damage level is indicated by a color code.  Damage levels 1-10 are shown in green, levels 11-19 are shown in salmon, levels 20-49 are shown in orange and levels 50 and above are shown in red.
Damage Flt – Current flotation damage level.  The severity of the damage level is indicated by a color code.  Damage levels 1-10 are shown in green, levels 11-19 are shown in salmon, levels 20-49 are shown in orange and levels 50 and above are shown in red.
Damage Eng – Current damage level of the ships power plant.  The severity of the damage level is indicated by a color code.  Damage levels 1-10 are shown in green, levels 11-19 are shown in salmon, levels 20-49 are shown in orange and levels 50 and above are shown in red.
Damage Fires – Current level of ship fires.  The severity of the fires is indicated by a color code.  Fire levels 1-10 are shown in green, levels 11-19 are shown in salmon, levels 20-49 are shown in orange and levels 50 and above are shown in red.
Cargo Sup – Current amount of supplies carried as cargo.
Cargo Fuel – Current amount of fuel carried as cargo.
Cargo Troops – Current number of troops being transported.
